                                               ORDER
                                (Passed on this day of 7th March, 2024)

1. Madhya Pradesh Power Generation Company Ltd. (hereinafter called “the petitioner” or
   “MPPGCL”) has filed the subject petition for true-up of generation tariff for FY 2022-23
   determined by the Madhya Pradesh Electricity Regulatory Commission (hereinafter
   called “the Commission or MPERC”) vide Multi-Year Tariff order dated 19 th May, 2021 in
   petition No 53 of 2020 read with corrigendum dated 9th September, 2021.

2. The subject true-up petition has been filed by MPPGCL under Section 62 and Section 64
   of the Electricity Act, 2003, read with proviso 9.4 of MPERC (Terms and Conditions for
   Determination of Generation Tariff) Regulations, 2020, (hereinafter referred to as
   “Regulations, 2020”). The subject petition is based on the Annual Audited Accounts of
   MPPGCL for FY 2022-23.

3. The details of the power stations/houses covered in subject true-up petition are as given
   below:

4. Earlier, the petitioner filed petition No. 53 of 2020 for determination of Multi-year Tariff
   (MYT) for the control period FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24 based on the Regulations, 2020.
   Vide Order dated 19th May, 2021, the Commission determined the Multi-Year Tariff for
   the Control period of FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24 subject to True-Up based on Annual
   Audited Accounts for the respective year. Subsequently, a corrigendum to the aforesaid
   MYT Order was issued on 9th September, 2021.

6. In the subject true-up petition, the petitioner broadly submitted the following:
        a)     MPPGCL entered into Power Purchase Agreement (PPA) with MP Tradeco
               (now MPPMCL) on 29.11.2006 for existing power stations and on 04.01.2011
               for new Thermal Power Stations. Further, the first amendment to the Power
               Purchase Agreement (PPA) dated 29.11.2006 towards Purchase of Power
               from then existing and ongoing Thermal and Hydel Units of M. P. Power
               Generating Company Limited, has been signed on 18.07.2017 at Jabalpur.
               This First Amendment in Agreement provides term period till retirement/
               decommissioning of all Thermal and Hydel Power Stations of MPPGCL listed
               in the agreement. The PPAs for new power stations have also been amended
               from time to time. These PPAs provides that the tariff payable by Tradeco to
               Genco and terms & conditions related thereto shall be as determined by the
               State Commission. MPPGCL has accordingly been raising the bills of energy
               sold to Tradeco since 1.6.2005 as per the tariff determined by the Commission
               from time to time.
        b)     The Commission notified, MPERC (Terms & Conditions for determination of
               Generation Tariff) Regulations, 2020{RG-26 (IV) of 2020} on 28.02.2020. This
               regulation was based on multiyear tariff principles and incorporated norms of
               operation for control period FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24.
        c)     In the instant True up petition, MPPGCL has proposed true up for FY 2022-23
               in respect of existing/new stations whose tariff was determined by
               Commission vide Multi Year Tariff order dated 19.05.2021 for the control
               period FY 2019-20 to FY 2023-24 read with corrigendum to aforesaid MYT
               order vide order dated 09.09.2021.
        d)     MPPGCL has prepared the Books of Accounts for FY 2022-23 and got the
               same audited by the Statutory Auditor M/s J.S. Oberoi & Co., Chhindwara,
               Chartered Accountants. The Audit Officers from O/o Dy. Accountant General
               (ES-III), Bhopal, has conducted Supplementary Audit of Annual Statement of
               Accounts of MPPGCL for FY 2022-23. The Final Comments Certificate from
               AG Audit to MPPGCL is awaited.
        e)     MPPGCL is submitting the True up petition for FY 2022-23 based on the
               Audited Annual Statements of Accounts for FY 2022-23 under section 62 & 64
               of the Electricity Act 2003 read with proviso-9 of the Regulation, 2020.
        f)     The Annual Statement of Accounts for FY 2022-23 has been prepared on the
               basis of Indian Accounting Standards (IndAS) prescribed under Section 133
               of the Companies Act read with the rule 3 of Companies (Indian Accounting
               Standard) Rules, 2015 and Companies (Indian Accounting Standard)
               amendment Rules, 2016.


  M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 4
                                                        MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

        g)          The Petitioner has adopted following approach for True-up of Generation Tariff
                    for FY 2022-23: -
              i.      The Energy Charges (Variable Charges) has been billed in accordance with
                      proviso 18, 19 & 43 of the Regulation, 2020. Therefore, no truing up of
                      Energy Charges has been considered in this petition.
             ii.      Other Charges comprising of MPERC Fees, Water Charges, Rent, Rates
                      & taxes, Cost of Chemical & Consumable and Publication Expenses have
                      been claimed on actuals based on Audited Accounts for FY 2022-23.
             iii.     The expense shown in Audited Annual Statements of Accounts for FY
                      2022-23 pertains to MPPGCL’s share. The expenses, as extracted from
                      Audited Annual Statements of Accounts for FY 2022-23 for the shared
                      portion have been factored to represent 100% capacity operated by
                      MPPGCL to match with MPERC’s Multi Year Tariff Order dated 19.05.2021.
             iv.      The expenses of Rana Pratap Sagar and Jawahar Sagar indicated in the
                      Annual Statements of Accounts for FY 2022-23 of MPPGCL have not been
                      considered in this True up Petition since Commission has not considered
                      these projects in Tariff order, being operated by Rajasthan authorities.
             v.       At present, expenditure towards Pension & Terminal benefits of all the
                      Companies i.e. unbundled entities of MPSEB are being allowed in the MYT/
                      True up of Transmission Licensee i.e. MPPTCL on ‘pay as you go’ principle,
                      subject to true-up in each year on availability of actual figures. Accordingly,
                      MPPGCL has not claimed these expenses in this True-up tariff petition.

   Station-wise true-up amount for FY 2022-23:

 9. In the subject petition, the petitioner has filed additional capitalization of Rs. 153.88 Crore
    in thermal and hydel power stations (including HQ) during FY 2022-23 as per Annual
    Audited Accounts and Asset-cum-Depreciation registers of respective power stations.
    The petitioner also filed the write-off/ deductions of assets of Rs. 14.83 Crore in some of
    the power stations (including HQ) during FY 2022-23 as per Annual Audited Accounts
    and Asset-cum Depreciation Registers of respective power stations.

 10. With the above submissions, the petitioner prayed the following:
       (a)    Approve Annual Capacity (Fixed) Charges and Other charges for FY 2022-23
              and permit recovery of True up amount as per Para 17 & 18 in six equal monthly
              instalments.
       (b)    Allow additional capitalization as per audited Annual Statements of Accounts for
              2022-23 and accordingly permit additional Depreciation, RoE and Interest on
              Normative Loan/excess equity.
       (c)    Allow Other Charges comprising of MPERC Fees, Water Charges, Rent, Rates
              & taxes, Cost of Chemical & Consumable, Publication Expenses and EL
              encashment on actuals based on Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2022-23
       (d)    Allow additional expenses incurred towards Transportation of Ash amounting to
              Rs. 11.56 Crores.
       (e)    In accordance with proviso 9.11 of Regulations, 2020, allow interest on differential
              true-up amount, if any.

    Procedural History:
11. Motion hearing in the subject true up petition was held on 13th December, 2023, wherein
    the petition was admitted and petitioner was directed to serve copies of petition to all
    Respondents in the matter. The Respondents were also asked to file their responses on
    the petition within 15 days. The petitioner was asked to file rejoinder within a week,
    thereafter.

12. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023, the information gaps and
    requirements of additional details/documents in the subject petition were communicated
    to the petitioner seeking its comprehensive reply along with all relevant supporting
    documents by 15th January, 2024.

13. The public notices inviting comments/suggestions from stakeholders were published on
    4th January, 2024 in the following newspapers:
           (i)    Dainik Bhaskar, Bhopal (Hindi)
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 8
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

           (ii)     Patrika, Gwalior (Hindi)
           (iii)    Nai Duniya, Indore (Hindi)
           (iv)     Raj Express, Jabalpur (Hindi)
           (v)      Free Express, Bhopal (English)
     Last date of filing comment/objections/suggestions was 25th January, 2024.

14. By affidavit dated 15th January, 2024, the petitioner filed its response on some of the
    queries raised by the Commission and requested to grant additional time extension of 10
    days for submission of balance information. By affidavit dated 25th January, 2024, the
    petitioner filed its response to the balance information issues/queries raised by the
    Commission.

15. By affidavit dated 2nd February, 2024, Respondent No. 1 (M.P. Power Management Co.
    Ltd) filed its comments/ response on the subject petition.

16. By affidavit dated 9th February, 2024, the petitioner filed rejoinder on the response/
    comments filed by the Respondent No. 1. The petitioner’s response on each comment
    offered by the Respondent No.1 along with the observations is annexed as Annexure- I
    of this order.

17. Vide letter dated 19th January, 2024, comments from only one stakeholder, Shri Rajendra
    Agarwal were received in this matter. By affidavit dated 9 th February, 2024, the petitioner
    filed its reply to the aforesaid comments filed by the stakeholder. The petitioner’s reply
    on each comment/suggestion offered by the stakeholder along with the observations is
    annexed as Annexure-II with this Order.

18. The public hearing in the subject true-up petition was held on 13 th February, 2024 through
    video conferencing, wherein the representatives of the petitioner, Respondent No 1 and
    stakeholder appeared.

    Disclaimer for Rounding
19. In this Order, certain numbers as a whole, upto several decimal places have been
    rounded up or down. Therefore, there may be discrepancies between the totals of the
    individual numbers shown in the tables upto two decimal places and numbers given in
    the corresponding analysis in the text of this order

     considered in the petition is the Closing Gross Block as on 31st March, 2022 as admitted

     submitted that additional capitalization was carried out during FY 2022-23 in existing
     power stations. These asset additions were made on account of new assets capitalized

    Provisions in Regulation:
22. Regarding capital cost of the existing generating stations, Regulation 21.3 and 21.4 of
    the Regulations, 2020 provides as under:

     21.3 “The Capital cost of an existing project shall include the following:
       (i) the capital cost admitted by the Commission prior to 1.4.2019 duly trued up by
             excluding liability, if any, up to last true-up order issued by the Commission;
       (ii) additional capitalization and de-capitalization for the respective year of tariff as
             determined in accordance with these Regulations;
       (iii) capital expenditure on account of renovation and modernization as admitted by

   M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 10
                                                     MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

              the Commission in accordance with these Regulations;
         (iv) capital expenditure on account of ash disposal including handling and
              transportation facility;
         (v) capital expenditure incurred towards railway infrastructure and its augmentation
              for transportation of coal upto the receiving end of generating station but does not
              include the transportation cost and any other appurtenant cost paid to the railway;
              and
         (vi) capital cost incurred or projected to be incurred by a thermal generating station,
              on account of implementation of the norms under Perform, Achieve and Trade
              (PAT) scheme of Government of India shall be considered by the Commission
              subject to sharing of benefits accrued under the PAT scheme with the
              beneficiaries

      21.4 The capital cost in case of existing/new hydro generating station shall also include:
         (i) cost of approved rehabilitation and resettlement (R&R) plan of the project in
              conformity with National R&R Policy and R&R package as approved; and
         (ii) cost of the developer’s 10% contribution towards Rajiv Gandhi Grameen
              Vidyutikaran Yojana (RGGVY) and Deen Dayal Upadhyaya Gram Jyoti Yojana
              (DDUGJY) project in the affected area.”

     Commission’s Analysis:

     85/2022.

     station-wise Assets-cum-Depreciation registers for FY 2022-23. The petitioner was also
     asked to reconcile the figures of addition of assets recorded in the Assets-cum-
     Depreciation registers with the figures in Annual Audited Accounts for FY 2022-23 as well
     as with the figures filed in the subject true-up petition.

27. The petitioner submitted the power station-wise justification for the difference in opening
    GFA as on 01.4.2022 as filed in the petition vis-a-vis recorded in Annual Audited Accounts
    for FY 2022-23. From the above, it is observed that the difference in GFA of Rs. 25,173.97
    Crore (after not-considering HQs) as on 31st March, 2022 filed by the petitioner as per
    last true-up order dated 31st March, 2023 and the opening GFA as per Annual Audited
    Accounts for FY 2022-23 is on account of those assets which were not considered by the
    Commission in the previous tariff / true up orders due to various reasons such as not
    allowing asset additions over and above the cut-off date or capitalization not falling under
    the respective Regulations, etc., however, the petitioner has recorded all such asset
    additions in the Annual Audited Accounts.


     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                Page 12
                                                     MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

28. It was further observed that the total asset addition claimed in the subject petition is Rs.
    153.88 Crore, whereas, the total asset addition of Rs. 163.01 Crore is indicated in the
    Annual Audited Accounts. It is noted that the difference in additional capitalization of Rs.
    9.14 Crore is due to asset addition towards need based R&M works carried out in STPS
    PH-2&3. Since, the petitioner has availed special allowance in this Power Station,
    therefore the additional capitalization of Rs. 9.14 Crore in STPS PH-2&3 is not claimed
    by the petitioner.

29. It was also observed that the total deduction/write-off of assets filed in the petition is of
    Rs. 14.83 Crore whereas, the total deduction/write-off of Rs. 14.85 Crore is recorded in
    the Annual Audited Accounts. This mismatch of figures was observed in PH-2 & 3 of
    STPS Sarni. As per Annual Audited Accounts, write-off amount was Rs. 2.14 Crore in
    STPS PH-2 & 3 whereas, the write-off amount filed in the petition in this power station
    was Rs. 2.12 Crore. The petitioner has submitted that the deduction of assets of Rs. 0.02
    Crore pertains to assets capitalized in STPS PH-2&3 during FY 2013-14 as MPPGCL
    has not claimed the asset additions for said year due to availing of special allowance. As
    MPPGCL has opted for special allowance at STPS PH-2&3, no additional capitalization
    was considered by the Commission from FY 2012-13 onwards. Accordingly, adjustment
    of Rs. 0.02 Crore with respect to assets added during FY 2013-14 is not considered by
    the petitioner, while filing the write-off amount in the subject petition.

    Provision in Regulation
32. Regarding additional capitalization of the generating stations, Regulations 27.1, 27.2 and
    28.1 of the Regulations, 2020 provide that:

          27. Additional Capitalization within the original scope and after the cut-off date:
     27.1 The additional capital expenditure incurred or projected to be incurred in respect of
            an existing project or a new project on the following counts within the original scope
            of work and after the cut-off date may be admitted by the Commission, subject to
            prudence check:
     (i) Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the directions or order of
            any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law;
     (ii) Change in law or compliance of any existing law:
     (iii) Deferred works relating to ash pond or ash handling system including ash
            transportation facility in the original scope of work;
     (iv) Liability for works executed prior to the cut-off date;
     (v) Force majeure events;
     (vi) Liability for works admitted by the Commission after the cut-off date to the extent of
            discharge of such liabilities by actual payment; and
     (vii) Additional capitalization on account of raising of ash dyke as a part of ash disposal
            system.

     27.2 In case of replacement of assets deployed under the original scope of the existing
          project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted by the
          Commission, after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the
          cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check on the following grounds:
     (a) The useful life of the assets is not commensurate with the useful life of the project
          and such assets have been fully depreciated in accordance with the provisions of
          these Regulations;
     (b) The replacement of the asset or equipment if necessary, on account of change in
          law or Force Majeure conditions;
     (c) The replacement of such asset or equipment is necessary on account of
          obsolescence of technology; and
     (d) The replacement of such asset or equipment has otherwise been allowed by the
          Commission.

     28. Additional Capitalization beyond the original scope:
     28.1 The capital expenditure in respect of existing generating station incurred or
          projected to be incurred on the following counts beyond the original scope, may be
          admitted by the Commission, subject to prudence check:
     (a) Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions of
          the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law;

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 15
                                                         MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

     (b)       Change in law or compliance of any existing law;
     (c)       Force Majeure Events;
     (d)       Any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher security and
               safety of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or
               statutory authorities responsible for national security/ internal security;
     (e)       Deferred works relating to ash pond or ash handling system in addition to the original
               scope of work, on case to case basis:
               Provided that if any expenditure has been claimed under Renovation and
               Modernisation (R&M) or repairs and maintenance under O&M expenses, the same
               expenditure cannot be claimed under this Regulation; and
     (f)       Usage of water from sewage treatment plant in thermal generating station

33. With regard to additional capitalization, by affidavit dated 2 nd February, 2024, the
    Respondent No. 1 (MPPMCL) in its response on the subject petition has submitted the
    following:

               That, the Petitioner has claimed Additional Capitalization to the tune of Rs. 153.88
                crore. For the reasons that the Petitioner has not only given the figures in rupee
                terms in respect of additional capitalization relating to each of its projects but has
                failed to provide detailed justification of the same relating to the nature of capital
                asset, its necessity, etc., the claim in this respect needs to be rejected.
     Additional capitalization under Thermal Power Stations:

    Commission’s Analysis:
34. In the subject true-up petition, petitioner has filed additional capitalization of Rs.153.88
    Crore during FY 2022-23 in its thermal and hydel power stations. The Commission has
    examined Power Station/House-wise additional capitalization claimed by the petitioner in
    light of the Annual Audited Accounts, Asset-cum-Depreciation Registers, provisions
    under the Regulations, details and documents filed by the petitioner and the
    comments/suggestions filed by the Respondent No 1 and other stakeholders in this
    regard.

35. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023, the petitioner was asked to submit
    various details/documents regarding additional capitalization in its thermal and hydel
    power stations in terms of the Regulations, 2020. By affidavit dated 25 th January, 2024
    the petitioner filed its response on the issues raised by the Commission. Power
    Station/House wise analysis of the additional capitalization done by the Commission is
    as given below:

     a) Amarkantak Thermal Power Station (ATPS) PH-3:

     on the Annual Audited Accounts.

38. On scrutiny of the aforesaid details filed by the petitioner, it is observed that the
    generating unit of ATPS PH-3 achieved CoD on 10th September, 2009, therefore this
    power station is considered as existing project in terms of Regulation 3 (20) of the
    Regulations, 2020. The additional capital expenditure claimed in ATPS PH-3 are
    capitalized after the cut-off date of the unit. Therefore, the said additional capitalization
    has been analyzed in terms of Regulation 27 of the Regulations, 2020, which provides
    that “the additional capitalization within the original scope of work and after the cut-off
    date of the project may be admitted by the Commission, subject to prudence check”. It is

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                  Page 17
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

     also observed that the petitioner has claimed some of the assets under additional
     capitalization beyond the original scope of work, hence the aforesaid additional
     capitalization filed by the petitioner has also been analysed in terms of Regulation 28.1
     of the Regulations, 2020.

39. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023, the petitioner was asked to file
    certain details/ documents regarding additional capitalization with respect to ATPS PH-3.
    In response, by affidavit dated 25th January, 2024, the petitioner submitted the details
    regarding additional capitalization claimed in ATPS PH-3.

40. The additional capitalization claimed by the petitioner in ATPS PH-3 has been analysed
    under different heads of assets as given below:

        i. Drainage and Sewerage Residential Colony:-
           Petitioner’s Submission
           The Capitalization under Account Code 10.322 of Rs 0.27 Crores is towards
           Drainage And Sewerage Residential Colony vide Voucher No. 680294 dated
           31.03.2023 under the work order for the work of providing and laying of water
           pipeline for optimum utilization of treated effluent water from Sewage Treatment
           Plant (STP) to Various Gardens and Plantation area situated in colony area.
           The aforesaid capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(f) of MPERC
           Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Usage of water from sewage treatment plant
           in thermal generating station.

           Commission’s Analysis
           The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs. 0.27 Crore towards
           drainage and sewerage residential colony which has been capitalized in the Annual
           Audited Accounts for FY 2022-23. The aforesaid additional capitalization is claimed
           under Regulation 28.1 (f) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Usage of
           water from sewage treatment plant in thermal generating station”. On perusal of
           the details filed by the petitioner, it is observed that the aforesaid expenditure under
           additional capitalization is made after the cut-off date of the project and beyond the
           original scope of works. Such assets have been duly recorded in Asset-cum-
           Depreciation Register and Annual Audited Accounts, therefore, same is covered
           under Regulation 28.1 (f) of Regulations, 2020. Hence, the said expenditure is
           allowed in this Order.

       ii. Railway Sidings:-
           Petitioner’s Submission
           The Capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.412 of Rs 0.09 Crores is
           towards Railway Sidings works of construction of 02 Nos. Gate gumty and manual
           operated railway vertical gate at level crossing on private Railway siding vide

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 18
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

      Document no. 680266 dated 31.03.2023 read with M/s AKS. Construction works
      order No.70 dated 03.05.2022.
      Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 27.1(vi) of MPERC Regulations
      2020, which provides for “Liability of works admitted by the Commission after the
      cut-off date to the extent of discharge of such liabilities by actual payment.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed Rs 0.09 Crore towards Railway Siding under Regulation
      27.1 (vi) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides “any liability for works admitted by
      the Commission after the cut-off date to the extent of discharge of such liabilities by
      actual payments”. The petitioner has not demonstrated whether this work was taken
      into consideration by the Commission after the cut-off date or that it falls under the
      category of actual payment for the discharge of any such liabilities. However, the
      assets have been put to use and capitalized in FY 2022-23. It is observed that the
      capitalization made on account of railway sidings of Rs 0.09 Crore is not covered
      under Regulation 27.1 (vi) of the Regulations, 2020, since the Commission has not
      admitted any such works after the cut-off date, hence, this amount is not allowed in
      this Order.

 iii. Boiler Plant & Equipments:-
      The capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.501 is procurement of Voith
      make hydraulic coupling for Induced Draft (ID) Fan at 210 MW vide order No. 404
      dated 25.05.2021 amounting to Rs. 0.705 Crores and M/s BHEL make hydraulic
      adjustment device (Servo Motor) for Forced Draft (FD) fan Type-FAF 17/9.5-1 vide
      order No. 1035 dated 28.07.2021 amounting to Rs. 0.292 Crores. The above total
      capitalization works is amounting to Rs. 0.997 Crore approx.
      The aforesaid capitalization is claimed as per proviso 27.2(c) of MPERC
      Regulations, 2020 which provides for “The replacement of such asset or
      equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology”.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.997 Crore towards
      Boiler, plant & Equipments under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020
      which provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or
      equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation
      27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the
      original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization
      may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the
      gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

      In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
      expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (c) of
M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 19
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

      the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not justified the replacement of existing
      assets towards obsolescence of technology. The petitioner has also not
      demonstrated any adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative
      depreciation towards replacement of existing assets in its Asset-cum-Depreciation
      register as well as its Annual Audited Accounts. Hence, expenditure towards
      Boiler, Plant & Equipments is not allowed in this Order.

 iv. Auxilliaries in Steam Power Plant:-
     Petitioner’s Submission
     The capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.509 of Rs. 0.08 Crores is
     towards procurement of LT 3-Phase Squirrel cage Induction motors of different
     ratings vide order No. 1964 dated 14.08.2020 and spare motor of CW make-up
     pump ABB make motor vide order No. 684 dated 08.05.2014 and 220 V Dc motor
     vide order No. 3575 dated 17.12.2020.
     The aforesaid capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 27.2(c) of the Regulations,
     2020 which provides for “The replacement of such asset or equipment is necessary
     on account of obsolescence of technology.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.08 Crore towards
      auxiliaries in steam power plant under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020
      which provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or
      equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation
      27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the
      original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization
      may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the
      gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

      In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
      expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (c) of
      the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not justified the replacement of existing
      assets towards obsolescence of technology. The petitioner has also not
      demonstrated any adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative
      depreciation towards replacement of existing assets in its Asset-cum-Depreciation
      register as well as its Annual Audited Accounts. Hence, expenditure towards
      auxiliaries in steam power plant is not allowed in this Order.

  v. Instrumentation & Control:-
     Petitioner’s Submission
     The Capitalization under Account Code 10.520 amounting to Rs 0.13 Crores is
     supply of calibration kit for sensor CV-861 vide order No. 815 dated 25.05.2022 and
     supply of Battery Cell Booster for I&C Division vide order No. 527 dated 12.05.2020.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 20
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

      The said capitalization is claimed under Proviso 28.1(a) of the Regulations, 2020
      which provides for compliance of “ directions given by Statutory authorities”.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.13 Crore towards
      Instrumentation and Controls and has claimed under Regulation 28.1 (a) which
      provides for “Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or
      directions of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law”. On
      perusal of the details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed that the
      petitioner has not demonstrated that whether the aforesaid expenditure is made on
      advise or direction by the Govt. agency or any statutory body in accordance to the
      Regulations. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 0.13 Crore towards Instrumentation &
      Control is not allowed in this Order.

 vi. Transmission Plant- Transformers 100 kVA & Above: -
     Petitioner’s Submission
     The Capitalization under Account Code 10.541 amounting to Rs 1.85 Crores is
     supply, Erection, Testing & Commissioning of 20 MVA, 220 kV/ 33 kV Transformer

     The above-mentioned capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of
     CEA (Measures Relating to Safety and Electric Supply) Regulations, 2010 in page
     No. 16 is as reproduced below:
     “(a) every motor, generator, transformer and other electrical plant and
     equipment together with apparatus used for controlling or regulating the
     same.”
     Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations
     2020, which provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed Rs 1.85 Crore under additional capitalization towards
      transformers of power house under Regulation 28.1 (b) of Regulations, 2020 which
      provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work on
      account of Change in law or compliance of any existing law. The said expenditure
      of Rs 1.85 Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 28.1
      (b) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not demonstrated any change in
      law event or compliance with existing law in relation to aforesaid CEA Regulations.
      Hence, the additional capitalization of Rs 1.85 Crore towards works related to
      transmission plant-transformers is not allowed under Regulation 28.1 (b) in this
      Order.

      compliance to CEA Advisory dated 23.5.2022 regarding safety advisory to all
      thermal power generating utilities, hence, the aforesaid additional capitalization of
      Rs 0.44 Crore is allowed in this Order.

 xv. Capital Spares: -
     Petitioner’s Submission
     MPPGCL has conducted physical verification of Inventory and Valuation of Material
     at Site of its power stations. Based on said verification, MPPGCL has identified the
      minor amount of Capital Spares at ATPS PH-3 and the same have been capitalized
      in the Books of Accounts of FY 2022-23 amounting to Rs.0.08 Crores. . The same
      is claimed in the instant petition. The supporting documents in this regard, are
      submitted.

       Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 26.1 (iii) of MPERC Regulations,
      2020 which provides for Procurement of initial capital spares within the original
      scope of work, in accordance with the provisions of Regulation 25 of MPERC
      Regulations.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed capital spares of Rs 0.08 Crore under Regulation 26.1
      (iii) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides that “procurement of initial capital
      spares within the original scope of work upto cut-off date in accordance with the
      provisions of Regulation 25”. Since, the capital spares of Rs. 0.08 Crore are
      capitalized and claimed by the petitioner during FY 2022-23 after the cut-off date
      of the project, hence, this amount is not allowed under Regulation 26.1 (iii) in this
      Order at this stage.

xvi. Boiler/Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure:
     The Statutory compliance of Indian Accounting Standards (INDAS) is mandatory
     on MPPGCL. Based on said standards, the Expenditure on Major inspection/capital
      Overhaul- Boiler/ Turbine was capitalized in the Audited books of Accounts of
      MPPGCL in FY 2022-23 at ATPS, Chachai under Account Code 11.602 amounting
      to Rs 7.38 Crores Chachai & under Account Code 11.603 amounting to Rs 15.59
      Crores, totaling to Rs.22.97 Crores. The same is claimed in the instant petition. The
      supporting documents in this regard, are submitted.
      Aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations,
      20 which provides for “Additional capitalization on account of Change in law or
      compliance of any existing law.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 22.97 Crore towards
      Boiler/Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure under Regulation 28.1 (b) which
      provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law”. It is observed that

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 30
                                                       MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

           the aforesaid additional capitalization is only book entry, due to change in
           accounting practices under Indian Accounting Standards, whereas, the
           Commission is determining tariff under GAAP. The said expenditure of Rs. 22.97
           Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the
           Regulations, 2020. It is also observed that petitioner is unable to justify its claim of
           additional expenditure under the Regulations, 2020. Hence, this expenditure of Rs
           22.97 Crore is not allowed in this Order.

    this Order.

48. On perusal of the aforesaid details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed
    that the generating units of STPS PH-4 achieved CoD on 16.03.2014 and the Cut-off
    date of the project in terms of proviso 4.1 (l) of MPERC (Terms and Conditions for

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 32
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

     Determination of Generation Tariff) Regulations, 2015 was 31.03.2017. Therefore, this
     power station is considered as existing project in terms of Regulation 3.1(20) of the
     Regulations, 2020. The additional capitalization claimed in STPS PH-4 is after the cut-off
     date of the project. Therefore, aforesaid additional capitalization needs to be examined
     in light of the Regulation 27 of the Regulations, 2020. It is also observed that the petitioner
     has claimed some of the additional capitalization components beyond the original scope
     of work, hence, the said additional capitalization needs to be examined in light of the
     Regulation 28.1 of the Regulations, 2020.

49. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023 the petitioner was asked to file
    certain details/ documents regarding the aforesaid additional capitalization claimed in
    STPS PH-4. By affidavit dated 25th January, 2024, the petitioner submitted such details.

50. The additional capitalization claimed by the petitioner in STPS PH-4 has been analysed
    under different heads of assets separately as given below:

      i.   Railway Sidings
           Petitioner’s Submission
           The Capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.412 amounting to Rs 25.62
           Crores is towards Rail transportation system at Ghora Dongri yard & Sarni yard
           vide letter no. 945 dated 28.08.2023 by Central Railway. The aforesaid
           capitalization is covered under the Original Scope of Work.
           The Capital Expenditure was incurred towards Deposit Works for installation of Rail
           Transportation system at Ghoradongri Yard for STPS PH-4 Sarni & balance work
           including Signaling gears & Equipments replacement, loop line & Engine siding Line
           renovation. The works were awarded to M/s RITES Ltd (Undertaking of Govt. of
           India). M/s RITEs has got this work done from sub vendors/suppliers. These works
           have already been completed by M/s RITES Ltd.

           These are statutory works necessary for smooth and regular transportation of coal.
           The capital expenses towards the above work were lying in books of accounts as
           CWIP. After intimation from M/s RITES Ltd. they have been put to use and partially
           capitalized in FY 2018-19. The same was allowed by Commission in the True-up
           Order for FY 2018-19 at para 92, page No. 44 of Petition No. 02 of 2020. The said
           Deposit work of Railways was completed well within the cut-off date. The balance
           capitalization amounting to Rs. 25.62 Crores has been capitalized in FY 2022-23
           and has been claimed in the instant petition.

           The above mention Capitalization is claimed under following clauses of MPERC
           Regulations, 2020:-

                       “ 27.1 The capital expenditure, in respect of existing
                       generating station incurred or projected to be incurred on the
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 33
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

                  following counts after the cut-off date, may be admitted by
                  the Commission, subject to prudence check:
      (vi) Any liability for works admitted by the Commission after the cut-off date
      to the extent of discharge of such liabilities by actual payments.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed Rs. 25.62 Crore towards Railway Siding under
      Regulation 27.1 (vi) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides “any liability for works
      admitted by the Commission after the cut-off date to the extent of discharge of such
      liabilities by actual payments”. The petitioner mentioned that these expenses
      towards the rail transportation system works were lying in books of accounts as
      CWIP. After intimation from the railways, they have been put to use and was
      partially capitalized in FY 2018-19 which was admitted by the Commission in FY
      2018-19 in tariff order dated 29th April, 2021 in P. No 02 of 2020. Since the
      capitalization towards partial works was admitted in FY 2018-19, but balance
      capitalization amount was lying under CWIP in Annual Audited Accounts and after
      the assets have been put to use and got capitalized in FY 2022-23 after confirmation
      by Railways, hence, the capitalization made on account of railway sidings of Rs
      25.62 Crore is covered under Regulation 27.1 (vi) of the Regulations, 2020 after
      discharging of such liabilities by actual payment made by the petitioner. Therefore,
      the Commission has allowed the additional capitalization of Rs. 25.62 Crore
      towards railway sidings during FY 2022-23 under Regulation 27.1 (vi) in this Order.

ii.   Boiler Plant & Equipments:-
      Petitioner’s Submission
      The capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.501 is procurement of
      Fluidomate make hydraulic coupling SC-16 of Induced Draft (ID) Fan vide order No.
      1863 dated 26.03.2019 amounting to Rs. 0.80 Crore.
      The aforesaid capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 27.2(c) of the Regulations,
      2020 which provides for “The replacement of such asset or equipment is necessary
      on account of obsolescence of technology.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.80 Crore towards Boiler,
      Plant & Equipments under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 which
      provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or
      equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation 27.2
      further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
      scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be
      admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed
      assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 34
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023



       In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
       expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (c) of
       the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not justified the replacement of existing
       assets towards obsolescence of technology. The petitioner has also not
       justified/established any adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative
       depreciation towards replacement of existing assets in its Asset-cum-Depreciation
       register as well as its Annual Audited Accounts. Hence, expenditure towards Boiler,
       Plant & Equipments is not allowed in this Order at this stage.

iii.   Turbine-Generator-Steam Power Generation:-
       Petitioner’s Submission
       The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.503 amounting to Rs 9.03
       Crores is towards procurement of LP Turbine Free Standing Blades of “BHEL” make
       KWU Turbine set of vide order No. 1315 dated 07.01.2020. The order copies are
       annexed.
       The Central Electricity Authority (CEA) has issued advisory dated 07.02.2020 on
       Spares Part Management for all Thermal Power Utilities. The advisory provides list
       of critical Spares/ restoration Equipments to be maintained by Thermal Power
       Plants for counter any eventuality or stoppages. The copy of said Advisory is
       attached which includes above mentioned HPCV/ IPCV at page-8 of said document.
       Being statutory requirement, the said additional capitalization is claimed under
       proviso 28.1(a) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides for compliance of
       directions of statutory authority.
       MPPGCL humbly request Commission to kindly permit the same.

       Commission’s Analysis:
       The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 9.03 Crore towards
       procurement of turbine spares claimed under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides
       for “Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions
       of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law”. The petitioner
       submitted that the Central Electricity Authority (CEA) had issued advisory dated
       7.2.2020 on Spares Part Management for all Thermal Power Utilities. The advisory
       provided list of all critical spares/restoration equipments to be maintained by
       Thermal Power Plants for countering any eventuality or stoppages. Hence,
       procurement of these assets falls under statutory requirement. Therefore, the said
       expenditure of Rs 9.03 Crore claimed by the petitioner towards procurement of
       turbine spares is allowed in this Order under Regulation 28.1 of the Regulations,
       2020.

iv.    Coal Handling Plant & Handling Equipments: -
       Petitioner’s Submission

                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

      The additional capitalization booked under Account Code 10.515 amounting to
      Rs.0.95 Crores is towards procurement of Hydraulic Motor of Wagon Trippers &

      17.12.2018.
      Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1 (b) of MPERC Regulations,

      The petitioner has claimed Rs 0.95 Crore under additional capitalization towards

      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.15 Crore towards
      Instrumentation & Controls claimed under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for
      “Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions
      of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law”. On perusal of
      the details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed that the petitioner
      has not demonstrated that whether the aforesaid expenditure is made on advise or
      direction by the Govt. agency or any statutory body in accordance to the
      Regulations. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 0.15 Crore towards Instrumentation &
      Controls is not allowed in this Order at this stage.

      The additional capitalization of CCTV is claimed under proviso 28.1 (d) of MPERC
      Regulations 2020, which provides for “Any capital expenditure to be incurred on
      account of need for higher security and safety of the plant as advised or directed by
      appropriate Government Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for national
      security/ internal security".
      Further, the capitalization of other office Equipments is claimed under proviso
      27.2(c) of the Regulations 2020, which provides for “The replacement of such asset
      or equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology”.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.03 Crore towards office
      equipments under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides that
      the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment is
      necessary on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation 27.2 further
      provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original scope
      of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted
      by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets
      and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

      it is observed that the said expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under
      the Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the
      justification of replacement of existing assets towards obsolescence of technology.
      The Commission is of the view that the additional capital expenditure (ACE)
      incurred by the petitioner towards office equipments is in the nature of Operation &
      Maintenance Expenses. Hence, expenditure towards Office equipments is not
      allowed in this Order.

      Further, the petitioner also claimed additional capitalization towards purchase of
      CCTV Cameras of Rs 0.03 Crore under Regulation 28.1 (d) of the Regulations,
      2020. Regulation 28.1 (d) provides that “for any capital expenditure to be incurred
      on account of need for higher security and safety of the plant as advised or directed
      by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for
      national security/ internal security”. However, the petitioner has not submitted any
      advisory/directive by appropriate Government Agencies or Statutory Authorities
      responsible for national security/ internal security, therefore, these expenses are

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 38
                                                  MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

        not allowed in this Order.

viii.   Capital Spares:-
        The capital spares booked under Account Code 11.300 amounting to Rs.0.04
        Crores are capitalized in FY 2022-23 in the Audited Books of Accounts of FY 2022-
        23 and same is considered in the instant True up petition.
        The above mentioned Capitalization is claimed under proviso 27.1(vi) following
        clauses of MPERC Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Any liability for works
        admitted by the Commission after the cut-off date to the extent of discharge of such
        liabilities by actual payments;” read with proviso 25 of the Regulations, 2020
        towards ceiling limit of capital spares. The copy of store indent is submitted.

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.04 Crore towards capital
        spares under Regulation 27.1 (vi) which provides for “liability for works admitted by
        the Commission after the cut-off date to the extent of discharge of such liabilities by
        actual payments”. It is observed that such spares have been capitalized after the
        cut-off date, therefore, the capital spares of Rs. 0.04 Crore claimed by the petitioner
        during FY 2022-23 is not covered under Regulation 27 (vi) of the Regulations, 2020.
        Hence, this amount is not allowed in this Order.

 ix.    Boiler Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure:-

        petition.

        compliance of any existing law”.

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs. 1.49 Crore towards
        Boiler/Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure under Regulation 28.1 (b), which
        provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law”. It is observed that
        the aforesaid additional capitalization is only book entry due to change in accounting
        practices under Indian Accounting Standards, whereas the Commission is
        determining tariff under GAAP. The said expenditure of Rs 1.49 Crore claimed by
        the petitioner does not fall under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. It is

 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 39
                                                       MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

           also observed that petitioner is unable to justify its claim of additional expenditure
           under the Regulations, 2020. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 1.49 Crore is not
           allowed in this Order.

55. On perusal of the details and documents filed by the petitioner, the Commission has
    observed that above assets have been capitalized in the Annual Audited Accounts of
    MPPGCL for FY 2022-23 and these assets are also recorded in Assets-cum-depreciation
    Register. SGTPS PH-1&2 were declared under commercial operation prior to
    01.04.2020, therefore this power station is considered as existing project in terms of
    Regulation 3.1 (20) of the Regulations, 2020. The additional capitalization filed by the
    petitioner is after cut-off date of the project, therefore, same has been analysed in terms
    of Regulation 27 of the Regulations, 2020, which provides that “the capital expenditure,
    in respect of existing generating station incurred or projected to be incurred on the several
    counts after the cut-off date, may be admitted by the Commission, subject to prudence
    check”.

    Regulations, 2020.

58. The additional capitalization claimed by the petitioner in SGTPS PH-1&2 has been
    analysed under different heads of assets separately as given below :

       i.         Ash Handling Plant:-
                  Petitioner’s Submission
                  The additional capitalization booked under Account Code 10.507 is towards
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                       Page 41
                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

      Coupling for ash slurry pump amounting to Rs 0.29 Crores vide voucher dated
      29.10.2020.
      Aforesaid asset was transferred from ATPS PH-3 in FY 2017-18. In the True-Up
      order issued by Commission for FY 2017-18 dated 19.07.2019 in table 8, at page
      No. 10, it can be seen that Rs 6.25 Crores have been transferred from ATPS PH-3,
      Chachai to SGTPS Birsinghpur. However, all the transferred assets were not put to
      use in said Financial year. From the aforesaid table it is evident that out of Rs
      assets transferred 6.25 Crores. only assets Rs 2.75 Crores have been put to use in
      FY 2017-18 at SGTPS Birsinghpur. Out of above transfer, in FY 2022-23, Motor for
      Ash Slurry Pump amounting to Rs 0.29 Crores has been put to use in SGTPS, PH-
      1&2 and accordingly the same is considered in the instant petition.
      MPPGCL humbly request Commission to kindly permit the same.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner claimed the additional capitalization amounting to Rs. 0.29 Crore
      towards transfer of asset related to Ash Handling Plant from ATPS Chachai to
      SGTPS PH-1&2. As consequent impact, the amount of Rs. 0.29 Crore has already
      been considered under total asset deduction of Rs. 1.76 Crores at ATPS Chachai
      as reflected in corresponding table of subject True up petition. Correspondingly
      Normative Loan/Equity has also been reduced for ATPS Chachai, which is also
      reflected in subject true up petition. It is observed that the aforesaid additional
      capitalisation is towards book adjustment. Earlier the assets of Ash Handling Plant
      were under ATPS Chachai and now it has been transferred to SGTPS PH-1&2.
      Hence, the asset of Rs 0.29 Crore transfer from ATPS PH-3 to SGTPS PH-1&2 is
      allowed in this Order, accordingly.

       different relay other equipments viz. DCS ATRS GRP DAVR etc. and event recorder

       towards compliance of IEGC and MPEGC (Clause 6.1.9), towards safety/Protection
       of Generator and Electrical Grid System. The Central Electricity Authority regularly
       monitors the same. Being statutory requirement, the said additional capitalization
       of Rs 0.09 Crore is allowed in this Order under proviso 28.1(a) of the Regulations,
       2020, which provides for compliance of directive of statutory authority.

iii.   Tools & Tackles:-
       Petitioner’s Submission
       The capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.583 amounting to Rs. 0.25
       Crores is towards:-
               i.    Procurements of Latest Technology Portable vibration meter for
                     industrial use vide order No. 99 dated 20.04.2022 amounting to Rs.
                     0.014 Crores.
              ii.    Supply, Installation & Commissioning of 02 sets of Thermal Conductivity
                     Analyzer for Hydrogen Plant vide order no. 2572 dated 18.11.2021
                     amounting to Rs. 0.233 Crores.
       The order copies of above are submitted.
       The aforesaid capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC
       Regulations, 2020 which provides for change in law or compliance of any existing
       law and Proviso 27.2(c) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides for replacement of
       such asset or equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology.

       Commission’s Analysis:
       The petitioner has claimed Rs 0.25 Crore under additional capitalization towards
       other miscellaneous equipments under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020
       which provides for change in law or compliance of any existing law. The said
       expenditure of Rs 0.25 Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the
       Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. It was also observed that petitioner
       was unable to justify its claim of additional expenditure after the cut-off date under
       the Regulations, 2020. Hence, the aforesaid additional capitalization is not allowed
       in this Order.

       The petitioner has also claimed aforesaid additional capitalization towards tools and
       tackles under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides that the
       additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or equipment is necessary
       on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in
       case of replacement of assets deployed under the original scope of the existing
       project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted by the

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 43
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

      Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the
      cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

      In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
      expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (c) of
      the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not justified the replacement of existing
      assets towards obsolescence of technology. The petitioner has also not
      justified/established any adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative
      depreciation towards replacement of existing assets in its Asset-cum-Depreciation
      register as well as its Annual Audited Accounts. Hence, expenditure towards tools
      and tackles is not allowed in this Order.

iv.   Chemical Lab Equipments: -
      Petitioner’s Submission
      The Capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.588 amounting to Rs 0.08
      Crores is towards procurement of portable flue gas analyzer for division of energy
      efficiency cells vide order No. 817 dated 27.05.2022.
      This capitalization is made towards compliance of directives of CEA (Technical
      Standards for Construction of Electrical Plants and Electric Lines), Regulations,
      2010 mentioned in page No. 35 which is as under:-

      “(10) Laboratories-The Station shall be provided with following laboratories:
      (c) Chemical laboratories with necessary equipment, instruments and reagents for
      chemical analysis in water treatment plant, steam & water analysis and. analysis of
      coal, ash & flue gas.”

      Aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations,
      2020 which provides for change in law or compliance of any existing law.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.08 Crore towards
      Chemical lab equipments under Regulation 28.1 (b), which provides for “change in
      law or compliance of any existing law”. The petitioner stated that aforesaid
      expenditure was made towards compliance of directives of CEA (Technical
      Standards for Construction of Electrical Plants and Electric Lines), Regulations,
      2010, therefore, the same is covered under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations,
      2020. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 0.08 Crore is allowed in this Order.

v.    Furniture And Fixtures : -
      Petitioner’s Submission
      The Capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.800 amounting to Rs 0.0018
      Crores is towards procurement of Air Coolers vide order No. 427 dated 01.06.2018.
      The said capitalization is claimed Proviso 27.2(c) of MPERC Regulations, 2020
M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 44
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

      which provides for replacement of such asset or equipment is necessary on account
      of obsolescence of technology.

      Commission’s Analysis
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.0018 Crore towards
      Furniture and Fixtures under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020, which
      provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of assets or
      equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation 27.2
      further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original
      scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be
      admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed
      assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check”.

      In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
      expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (c) of
      the Regulations, 2020, as the petitioner has not shown the justification of
      replacement of existing assets towards obsolescence of technology. The
      Commission is of the view that the additional capital expenditure (ACE) incurred by
      the petitioner towards furniture & fixtures is in the nature of Operation &
      Maintenance Expenses. Hence, expenditure towards Furnitures & Fixtures is not
      allowed in this Order at this stage.

      project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted by the
      Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the
      cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

      In view of above provision under the Regulation, it is observed that the said
      expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the
      Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown justification of replacement of

        existing assets towards obsolescence of technology. The Commission is of the view

           Crore is not allowed in this Order.

    be treated as normative loan. Therefore, the Commission has considered normative debt
    equity ratio of 70:30 as per Regulations, 2020 in this Order.

64. SGTPS PH-3 was declared under commercial operation on 28.8.2008 prior to
    01.04.2019, therefore this power station is considered as existing project in terms of
    Regulation 3.1 (20) of the Regulations, 2020. The additional capitalization filed by the
    petitioner has been analysed in terms of Regulation 27 of the Regulations, 2020, which
    provides that “The capital expenditure, in respect of existing generating station incurred
    or projected to be incurred on the several counts after the cut-off date, may be admitted
    by the Commission, subject to prudence check”

65. It is observed that the petitioner has also claimed some of the additional capitalization
    components beyond the original scope of work, hence, the said additional capitalization
    needs examination in light of the Regulation 28.1 of the Regulations, 2020.

66. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023, the petitioner was asked to file

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                               Page 49
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

  certain details/ documents regarding aforesaid additional capitalization. By affidavit dated
  25th January, 2024, the petitioner submitted the required details. The additional
  capitalization claimed by the petitioner in SGTPS PH-3 has been analysed under different
  heads of assets separately as given below:-

i.    Pucca Roads: -
       Petitioner’s Submission
      The Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change on 31.12.2021 has
      issued Notification for on Ash Utilization from Coal or Lignite Thermal Power
      Stations (Change in Law). Wherein every Coal or lignite based thermal Power
      plants is primarily responsible to ensure 100% utilization of Ash generated by it in
      an eco-friendly manner. The earlier notification dated 25.01.2016 at Para 2(b) (9)
      had categorically mentioned that every coal or lignite based thermal power plants
      shall install dedicated dry ash silos having separate access road so as to ease
      delivery of fly ash. In compliance to the above RCC road was got constructed from
      ash silo I to IV to west gate of NTH and road from Gate No. 2 to MCR 500 MW in
      CHP area vide order No. 2665 dated 31.03.2021.
      The additional Capitalization has been booked under Account Code 10.401
      amounting to Rs 4.21 Crores.
      The said capitalization is being claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC
      Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Change in law or compliance of any existing
      law”. MPPGCL requests Commission to kindly permit the same.
      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed capitalization of Rs 4.21 Crore towards RCC road
      which was constructed from ash silo I to IV to west gate of NTH and road from
      Gate No. 2 to MCR 500 MW in CHP area so as to ease delivery of fly ash.
      Aforesaid additional capitalization is claimed under Regulation 27.1 (iii) and
      Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. Regulation 27.1 (iii) provides for
      deferred works relating to ash pond or ash handling system including ash
      transportation facility in the original scope of work after cut-off date and Regulation
      28.1(b) provides for additional capital expenditure beyond original scope of work
      on account of Change in law or compliance of any existing law. Since, the
      aforesaid capitalization is related to ash ponds works and has been done towards
      compliance of MoEF&CC notification towards Ash Utilization, hence, additional
      capitalization of Rs 4.21 Crore towards Pucca Roads is allowed in this Order under
      Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020.
ii.   Ash Handling Plant:-
      Petitioner’s Submission
      The additional Capitalization claimed under Account Code 10.507 amounting to
      Rs 0.20 Crore is towards Ash slurry pump motor transferred from ATPS, Chachai.

  M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 50
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

       Aforesaid asset was transferred from ATPS PH-3 in FY 2017-18. In the True-Up
       order issued by Commission for FY 2017-18 dated 19.07.2019 in table 8, at page
       No. 10, it can be seen that Rs 6.25 Crore have been transferred from ATPS PH-
       3, Chachai to SGTPS Birsinghpur. However, all the transferred assets were not
       put to use in said financial year. From the aforesaid table it is evident that out of
       assets transferred of Rs 6.25 Crore, only assets of Rs 2.75 Crores have been put
       to use in FY 2017-18 at SGTPS Birsinghpur. Out of above transfer, in FY 2022-
       23, Motor for Ash Slurry Pump amounting to Rs 0.20 Crore has been put to use in
       SGTPS, PH-3 and accordingly the same is considered in the instant petition.
       MPPGCL humbly requests Commission to kindly permit the same”.
       Commission’s Analysis:
       The petitioner claimed the additional capitalization amounting to Rs. 0.20 Crore
       towards transfer of asset related to Ash slurry pump motor transferred from ATPS,
       Chachai to SGTPS PH-3. As consequent impact, the amount of Rs. 0.20 Crore
       has already been considered under total asset deduction of Rs. 6.25 Crores at
       ATPS Chachai PH-3 which was considered by the Commission for FY 2017-18
       vide Order dated 19th July, 2019 in Table 8. However, all the transferred assets
       were not put to use in the said FY 2017-18, only Rs 2.75 Crore were been put to
       use in FY 2017-18 at SGTPS, Birsinghpur. Out of above transfer, in FY 2022-23,
       Motor for Ash Slurry Pump amounting to Rs 0.20 Crore have been put to use in
       SGTPS PH-3. It is observed that the aforesaid additional capitalisation is towards
       book adjustment. Earlier the Motor for Ash Slurry Pump was under ATPS and now
       it has been transferred to SGTPS, Birsinghpur. Hence, the asset transfer of Rs.
       0.20 Crore from ATPS PH-3 to SGTPS, Birsinghpur is allowed in this Order,
       accordingly.

           b) Supply of 1 No. Desktop PC for M-clubbed and Smart 2000 meter
               reading software for energy meter reading vide order No. 587 dated
               04.10.2021 amounting to Rs 0.007 Crores.

           c) Supply of 2 Nos. Laptops and 1 No. Auto Dulex Printer vide order No.
              91 dated 02.03.2022 amounting to Rs 0.01 Crores.

   Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 27.2(c) of MPERC Regulations
   2020, which provides for “The replacement of such asset or equipment is
   necessary on account of obsolescence of technology”.

   MPPGCL wishes to submit that any write-off towards replacement etc. carried out
   in future years shall be dealt in accordance with the Regulations and due care
   shall be taken to record the same in respective True up petitions.
   Commission’s Analysis:
   The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.03 Crore towards
   Furniture & Fixtures, Rs 0.04 Crore towards Computers under Regulation 27.2 (c)
   of the Regulations, 2020 which provides that the additional capitalization towards
   replacement of assets or equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of
   technology. Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets
   deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the
   additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making
   necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation,
   subject to prudence check.”
   In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
   expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (c)

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 54
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

         of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown justification of the
         replacement of the existing assets towards obsolescence of technology. The
         Commission is of the view that the additional capital expenditure (ACE) incurred
         by the petitioner towards Furniture & Fixtures & computers is in the nature of
         Operation & Maintenance Expenses. Hence, expenditure towards Furniture &
         Fixtures & computers is not allowed in this Order.
  vii.   Capital Spares:-
         Petitioner’s Submission
         The capital spares booked under Account Code 11.300 amounting to Rs.0.20
         Crores towards procurement of Capital Spares.
         Aforesaid capitalization is claimed as per proviso 26.1(iii) which provides for
         Procurement of initial capital spares within the original scope of work, read with
         proviso 25 of MPERC Regulations 2020 towards ceiling limit of Capital spares.
         Commission’s Analysis:
         The petitioner has claimed capital spares of Rs 0.20 Crore under Regulation 26.1
         (iii), which provides that “procurement of initial capital spares within the original
         scope of work upto cut-off date in accordance with the provisions of Regulation
         25”. Since, the capital spares of Rs. 0.20 Crore are capitalized in Annual Audited
         Accounts and claimed by the petitioner during FY 2022-23 are after the cut-off
         date of the project. This amount is not covered under the scope of Regulation 26.1
         (iii), hence, not allowed in this Order.
67. It is observed that the additional capitalization of Rs. 4.52 Crore is covered under the
    Regulations, 2020, hence, allowed in this Order. The break-up of assets addition
