74. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023, the petitioner was asked to file
    certain details/ documents regarding the additional capitalization in SSTPP PH-1. By
    affidavit dated 25th January, 2024, the petitioner filed its response. The additional
    capitalization claimed by the petitioner in SSTPP PH-1 has been analysed under different
    heads of assets separately as given below :

     i.   Other Buildings:-
          Petitioner’s Submission
          The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.233 amounting to Rs 5.80
          Crores is pertains to other Buildings is towards:-
            i.   Construction of store shed for hazardous material with chain link fencing
                 vide order No. 2267 dated 12.02.2021 amounting to Rs 0.66 Crores.
           ii.   Construction of limited height subway in lieu of level crossing at six
                 locations LC No.STP1 to STP6 in between Bir to Plants by cut & cover
                 method vide UO. No. 755 dated 26.04.2019 amounting to Rs 5.14 Crores.
          The MP Pollution board Notification provides for works to be carried out at
          the power Station for safety against hazardous material.
          Further as per directives of Indian railways (Western Central railways) the works
          towards limited height subway against level crossing was carried out for safety of
          railway property and general public.
          The said capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(d) of MPERC Regulations,
          2020 which provides for any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need
          for higher security and safety.

          Commission’s Analysis:
          The petitioner has claimed Rs 5.80 Crore under additional capitalization towards
          construction of other buildings. The said expenditure of Rs 5.80 Crore claimed by
          the petitioner under Regulation 28.1 (d) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 57
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

      for any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher security
      and safety of the plant. The petitioner has submitted that the aforesaid additional
      assets have been capitalized in Annual Audited Accounts and duly recorded in
      Asset-cum-Depreciation Register of the Project. Since, the aforesaid
      capitalization is done in compliance to MP Pollution board Notification provides
      for works to be carried out at the power Station for safety against hazardous
      material. Further as per directives of Indian railways (Western Central railways),
      the works towards limited height subway against level crossing was carried out
      for safety of railway property and general public, hence, the aforesaid additional
      capitalization of Rs 5.80 Crore is allowed under Regulation 28.1 (d) of the
      Regulations, 2020 in this Order.

ii.   Turbine Generator & Steam Power Generation :
       Petitioner’s Submission
      The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.503 amounting to Rs 0.03
      Crores procurement of Main Plants & Equipments of ’Main Power Block’ broadly
      comprising of BGT, Electricals and C&I packages including mandatory spares by
      M/s BHEL of amounting to Rs 0.03 Crores vide order No. 2907 dated 12.12.2008.
      Aforesaid order copies are annexed for kind reference please.
      The Central Electricity Authority (CEA) has issued advisory dated 07.02.2020
      on Spares Part Management for all Thermal Power Utilities. The advisory
      provides list of critical Spares/ restoration Equipments to be maintained by
      Thermal Power Plants for counter any eventuality or stoppages. The copy of said
      Advisory is attached which includes above mentioned HPCV/ IPCV at page-8 of
      said document.
      Being statutory requirement, the said additional capitalization is claimed under
      proviso 28.1(a) of the Regulations, 2020, which provides for compliance of
      directions of statutory authority.
      MPPGCL requests Commission to kindly permit the same.

      Commission’s Analysis:
      The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.03 Crore towards
      procurement of turbine spares under Regulation 28.1 (a) which provides for
      “Liabilities to meet award of arbitration or for compliance of the order or directions
      of the any statutory authority, or order or decree of any court of law”. The
      petitioner submitted that the Central Electricity Authority (CEA) had issued
      advisory dated 7.2.2020 on Spares Part Management for all Thermal Power
      Utilities. The advisory provided list of all critical spares/restoration equipments to
      be maintained by Thermal Power Plants for countering any eventuality or
      stoppages. The petitioner has submitted that the aforesaid additional assets have
      been capitalized in Annual Audited Accounts and duly recorded in Asset-cum-
      Depreciation Register of the Project. Hence, procurement of these assets falls

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                              Page 58
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

       under statutory requirement. Therefore, the said expenditure of Rs. 0.03 Crore
       claimed by the petitioner towards procurement of turbine spares is allowed in this
       Order under Regulation 28.1 of the Regulations, 2020.

iii.   Fabrication Shop/Workshop Plant & Equipments:-
       Petitioner’s Submission
       The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.565 amounting to Rs. 0.31

              03.03.2022.

       Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(b) of the Regulations 2020,
       which provides for change in law or compliance of any existing law.

       Commission’s Analysis:
       The petitioner has claimed Rs. 0.31 Crore under additional capitalization towards
       Fabrication shop/Workshop Plant & Equipments under Regulation 28.1 (b) of
       Regulations, 2020 which provides for additional capital expenditure incurred
       beyond original scope of works under change in law or compliance of any existing
       law. The said expenditure of Rs 0.31 Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall
       under the Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not
       demonstrated any change in law event or compliance with existing law in this
       regard. It was also observed that petitioner was unable to justify its claim of
       additional expenditure under the Regulations, 2020. Hence, the aforesaid
       additional capitalization is not allowed in this Order at this stage.

     2010 mentioned in page No. 34 which is as under:-
     (7) “Ventilation and air-conditioning system:
     (a) Suitable ventilation and air-conditioning system shall be provided to
     achieve proper working environment in the Station.”
     Aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 27.1 (vi) of MPERC

     The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization towards A/C and Ventilation

     which provides that any liability for works admitted by the Commission after the
     cut-off date to the extent of discharge of such liabilities by actual payments. On

    The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.905 amounting to Rs 0.04

    The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.001 Crore towards
    Furniture & Fixtures, Rs 0.09 Crore towards office Equipments and Rs 0.04 Crore

    equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology. Regulation
M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 62
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

        27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the
        original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization
        may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the
        gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

        In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
        expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 27.2 (c)
        of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the justification of
        replacement of existing assets towards obsolescence of technology. The
        Commission is of the view that the additional capital expenditure (ACE) incurred
        by the petitioner towards Furniture & Fixtures, Other equipments & computers is
        in the nature of Operation & Maintenance Expenses. Hence, expenditure towards
        Furniture & Fixtures, Other equipments & computers is not allowed in this Order.

        Further, the amount of additional capitalization towards purchase of CCTV
        Cameras of Rs. 0.04 Crore is not allowed under Regulation 28.1 (d) of the
        Regulations, 2020 in this Order because the petitioner has not submitted any
        advisory/directive by appropriate Government Agencies or Statutory Authorities
        responsible for national security/ internal security.

viii.   Capital Spares:-
        Petitioner’s Submission
        The capital spares booked under Account Code 11.300 amounting to Rs.0.15
        Crores towards procurement of capital Spares. Aforesaid capitalization is claimed
        as per proviso 26.1(iii) which provides for Procurement of initial capital spares
        within the original scope of work, read with proviso 25 of MPERC Regulations
        2020 towards ceiling limit of Capital spares.

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The Petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.15 Crore towards
        capital spares. The capitalization of aforesaid capital spares claimed under
        Regulation 26.1 (iii) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides that “additional
        capitalization towards procurement of initial capital spares within the original
        scope of work upto cut-off date in accordance with the provisions of Regulation
        25 of these Regulations”. Since, the capital spares of Rs. 0.15 Crore capitalized
        and claimed by the petitioner are after the cut-off date of the project, hence, this
        amount is not allowed under Regulation 26.1 (iii) in this Order.

 ix.    Capital Overhaul-Turbine/Generator.
         Petitioner’s Submission
        The Statutory compliance of Indian Accounting Standards (INDAS) is mandatory
        on MPPGCL. Based on said standards, the Expenditure on Major

   M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                              Page 63

          inspection/capital Overhaul-Turbine/generator was capitalized in the Audited
          books of Accounts of MPPGCL in FY 2022-23 at SSTPP Khandwa under Account
          Code 11.603 amounting to Rs. 1.03 Crores. The same is claimed in the instant
          petition.
          Aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC
          Regulations, 2020 which provides for change in law or compliance of any existing
          law.

          Commission’s Analysis:
          The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 1.03 Crore towards
          Capital Overhaul-Turbine Generator. The petitioner has claimed this component
          under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 which provides that the
          additional capitalization under “change in law or compliance of any existing law.
          The Commission is determining tariff/true up based on the GAAP Accounting
          standards, therefore, the additional capitalization claimed by the petitioner on
          account of change in accounting standards is not allowed in this Order for the
          purpose of true-up of tariff. Hence, the said expenditure of Rs. 1.03 Crore claimed
          by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations,
          2020 and is not allowed in this Order.

79. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023, the petitioner was asked to file
    certain details/ documents regarding the additional capitalization in SSTPP PH-2. By
    affidavit dated 25th January, 2023, the petitioner filed its response to the queries raised
    by the Commission as given below:

         Petitioner’s Submission
         The aforesaid additional capitalization has been covered under the Original
         Scope of Work Estimate of Rs.7820 Crore, which has been approved by Hon’ble
         GoMP. The copy of said approval has already been submitted before the
         Commission in the matter of determination of Final Generation Tariff of SSTPP
         Stage-II (Petition No. 25 of 2020).
         The Date of Commercial operation (CoD) of Unit No. 3 & 4 of SSTPP Stage-II
         (2x660 MW) Khandwa is 18.11.2018 & 27.03.2019 respectively. In accordance
         to the Regulations, 2020, the Cut-off date for the purpose of Additional
         Capitalization at SSTPP Stage-II (2x660 MW) Khandwa is 31.03.2022.
         The aforesaid capitalization is claimed under the provisos of MPERC
         Regulations, 2012 & MPERC Regulations, 2020.
         As desired by Commission, the information towards additional capitalization
         claimed in subject petition along with supporting documents (are submitted).
         It is requested before Commission to kindly permit the same.

Commission’s Analysis:
80. On scrutiny of the details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed that the
    Unit No. 4 of power station achieved CoD on 28th March, 2019, therefore, the Cut-off date
    of the Unit as per clause 4.1(l) of MPERC (Terms and Conditions for Determination of
    Generation Tariff) Regulations, 2015 is 31.03.2022. The petitioner has submitted that the
    aforesaid capitalization is claimed under Regulation 27 of Regulations, 2020 and
    reproduced sub clauses (iii), (v) and (vi) of Regulation 27.1 of the Regulations, 2020 in
    its additional submission. The petitioner also submitted that the aforesaid capitalization
    is within the original scope of work estimate approved by the Board of the Company.

81. Since, the additional capital expenditure filed by the petitioner in SSTPP PH-2 (2x660
    MW) is beyond the cut-off date of the power house and within the original scope of work,
    therefore, the same shall be examined in light of the Regulation 27 of the Regulations,
    2020.

82. On perusal of the details of additional capitalization of SSTPP PH-2, it is observed that
    the petitioner has not submitted component wise justification of the additional
    capitalization with relevant sub clauses of the Regulation 27.1 of the Regulations, 2020.
    The petitioner has only submitted that the additional assets capitalized in SSTPP PH-2

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 66
                                                     MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

     during FY 2022-23 are funded through PFC loan of Rs. 19.81 Crore and balance Rs.8.49
     Crore from Equity.

83. It is observed that the claim towards additional capitalization in SSTPP PH-2 is without
    any details and justifications in the subject petition as well as additional submissions
    placed on record by the petitioner, whereas, while claiming additional capitalization in
    other thermal and hydro power stations, the petitioner has mentioned asset-wise details
    and justifications in this regard. It seems that petitioner has adopted a very casual
    approach while claiming additional capitalization in SSTPP PH-2, therefore, the
    Commission is unable to identify under which sub clause of Regulation 27.1 of the
    Regulations, 2020, the additional capitalization is claimed by the petitioner. Further, the
    petitioner has also not demonstrated that whether the conditions specified under
    Regulation 27 is fulfilled by the petitioner.

84. However, the claim of Rs. 5.35 Crore towards Ash Handling Plant is covered under
    Regulation 27.1 (iii) which provides for deferred works relating to ash pond or ash
    handling system including ash transportation facility in the original scope of work after
    cut-off date. It is indicated that these are deferred works related to ash handling/disposal
    system works which is covered under the original scope of work, hence, additional
    capitalization of Rs 5.35 Crore towards Ash handling Plant Works is allowed in this Order
    under Regulation 27.1 (iii) of the Regulations, 2020.

85. In view of the above, the total additional capital expenditure of Rs. 5.35 Crore towards
    ash handling system which is within original scope of work is admitted in SSTPP PH-2
    during FY 2022-23 in accordance with the Regulations, 2020. For the balance assets
    capitalization, the petitioner is at liberty to approach the Commission with full details and
    justifications in next true-up petitions.

86. The petitioner has submitted that the additional capitalization has been funded through
    loan and equity component in the ratio of 70:30. Therefore, the Commission has
    considered same debt equity ratio of 70:30 as per Regulation, 2020 in this Order.

87. Accordingly, the details of the additional capitalization and funding considered in this
    Order are as given below:

        Batteries Including Charging Equipments:
        The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.563 amounting to Rs 0.05
        Crores is towards:
               a) Design, manufacture, supply, erection, testing & commissioning of 1

                   0.03 Crores vide order No. 3915 dated 02.12.2019 voucher No. 680080
                   dated 31.07.2022.
               b) Design, manufacture, supply, erection, testing & commissioning of
                   “Exide” make tubular battery set amounting to Rs 0.07 Crores vide order
                   No. 1904 dated 29.07.2020.
        Since the asset of Gandhisagar is shared in the ratio of 50%; so out of the total of
        Rs 0.10 Crores 50% is shared i.e the total asset added in FY 2022-23 is Rs 0.05
        Crores.
        This capitalization is carried out in compliance of directives of CEA (Technical
        Standards for Construction of Electrical Plants and Electric Lines), Regulations,
        2010 mentioned in page No. 59 reproduced as under:-
        “(6) DC supply system-

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                 Page 68
                                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

        (b) The battery shall have sufficient capacity to meet unit and station loads
        in addition to 3 hours of uninterrupted emergency illumination requirement.
        (c) DC system shall comprise of two DC battery sets (both battery sets of
        full capacity) each with one float cum boost charger.
        (d) The float cum boost battery charger as well as its automatic regulator
        shall be of static type. It shall have a facility of both auto as well as manual
        control in both the float and boost modes.”
        Aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC Regulations,
        2020 which provides for change in law or compliance of any existing law.

        The petitioner has claimed Rs 0.05 Crore under additional capitalization towards
        batteries including charging equipments under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the
        Regulations, 2020, which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond
        original scope of work on account of Change in law or compliance of any existing
        law. The said expenditure of Rs. 0.05 Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall
        under the Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not
        demonstrated any change in law event or compliance with existing law in relation
        to aforesaid CEA Regulations. Hence, additional capitalization of Rs. 0.05 Crore
        towards batteries including charging equipments is not allowed in this Order at this
        stage.

    the Regulations, 2020 in GandhiSagar HPS.

94. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023, the petitioner was asked to file
    certain details/ documents regarding the additional capitalization in Pench HPS. By
    affidavit dated 25th January, 2024 the petitioner filed its response to the queries. The
    additional capitalization claimed by the petitioner in Pench HPS has been analysed under
    different heads of assets separately as given below:

        i.    Hydel Power Generation Plant Equipments- Fire Protection System :-

              Petitioner’s Submission
              The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.531 amounting to Rs. 0.24
              Crores is towards Supply and installation of CO 2 based fire protection system
              vide order No.1469 dated 22.11.2022.
              This capitalization is carried out towards compliance of:-

                    1. Central Electricity Authority (Safety Requirements for Construction,
                       Operation and Maintenance of Electrical Plants and Electric Lines)
                       Regulations, 2011.
                    2. Central Electricity Authority (Measures relating to Safety and Electric
                       Supply) Regulations, 2010.
                    3. IS:3034 - Fire safety of industrial buildings: Electrical generating and
                         Distributing stations - Code of Practice.
                Further, CEA has released the Safety Advisory to all Thermal Power
                Generating Utilities on dated 23.05.2022.

                 Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(d) of MPERC
                 Regulations 2020, which provides for “Any capital expenditure to be incurred
                 on account of need for higher security and safety of the plant as advised or
                 directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory authorities
                 responsible for national security/ internal security”.

                 Commission’s Analysis:
                 The petitioner has claimed Rs. 0.24 Crore under additional capitalization
                 towards fire protection system. The said expenditure of Rs 0.24 Crore claimed
                 by the petitioner falls under Regulation 28.1 (d) of the Regulations, 2020 which
                 provides for any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for
                 higher security and safety of the plant. Since, the aforesaid capitalization is
                 done in compliance to CEA (Safety Requirements for Construction, Operation
                 and Maintenance of Electrical Plants and Electric Lines) Regulations, 2011,
                 (Measures relating to Safety and Electric Supply) Regulations, 2010 and
                 Advisory dated 23.5.2022 regarding safety advisory to all thermal power



     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                              Page 70
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

            generating utilities, hence, the aforesaid additional capitalization of Rs 0.24
            Crore is allowed under Regulation 28.1 (d) in this Order.

  ii.    Auxilliaries in Hydel Power Plants :-

           Petitioner’s Submission
           The capitalization booked under Account Code 10.535 amounting to Rs. 0.01
           Crores is towards :
           a) Supply of Heavy-duty submersible dewatering pump set for underground
               power house suitable for heavily contaminated water with sand, gravel &
               sludge with electrical control panel amounting to Rs. 0.01 Crores vide
               order No. 82 dated 15.01.2021.
           b) Supply of 3 Phase Induction motor amounting to Rs. 0.007 Crores vide order
             No. 144 dated 01.02.2022.
           The said capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 27.2(c) of the Regulations,
           2020 which provides for “The replacement of such asset or equipment is
           necessary on account of obsolescence of technology.

           Commission’s Analysis:
           The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.01 Crore towards
           heavy duty submersible dewatering pump and Rs 0.007 Crore towards 3-Phase
           transformers under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020, which
           provides for “The replacement of such asset or equipment is necessary on
           account of obsolescence of technology”. Further, Regulation 27.2 provides that
           “in case of replacement of assets deployed under the original scope of the
           existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization may be admitted
           by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed
           assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

           In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the
           said expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation
           27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the
           replacement of existing assets towards obsolescence of technology. The
           petitioner has also not justified/established any adjustments in the gross fixed
           assets and the cumulative depreciation towards replaced existing assets in its
           Asset-cum-Depreciation register as well as its Annual Audited Accounts.
           Hence, expenditure towards aforesaid additional capitalization is not allowed in
           this Order.

 iii.    Lightning Arrestors:-
         Petitioner’s Submission

                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

         The capitalization booked under Account Code 10.567 is towards procurement
         of Metal Oxide Gapless Lightening Arrestors for HV & EHV Sub-stations vide
         order No. 3100 dated 19.10.2022 amounting to Rs 0.002 Crore.

         Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(d) of MPERC Regulations
         2020, which provides for admittance of any capital expenditure to be incurred on
         account of need for higher security and safety of the plant as advised or directed
         by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory authorities.”
          Commission’s Analysis:
         The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs 0.002 Crore towards
         lightning arrestors under Regulation 28.1(d) of the Regulations, 2020, which
         provides any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher
         security and safety of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate
         Government Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for national
         security/ internal security”.
         On perusal of the details and documents filed by the petitioner, it is observed
         that the petitioner has not demonstrated that the aforesaid expenditure is
         made on advise or direction by the Govt. agency or any statutory body in
         accordance to the Regulations, 2020. Further, the aforesaid expenditure
         under additional capitalization is made after the cut-off date of the project,
         therefore, the same is not covered under the Regulation 28.1 (d) of the
         Regulations, 2020. Hence, the said expenditure of Rs 0.002 Crore is not
         allowed in this Order.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 72

                Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets
                deployed under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date,
                the additional capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after
                making necessary adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative
                depreciation, subject to prudence check.”
                In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the

                27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not justified the

       v.     Capital Overhaul- Turbine : -

                Aforesaid Capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC

                Boiler/Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure under Regulation 28.1 (b)
                which provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law”. It is
                observed that the aforesaid additional capitalization is only book entry due
                to change in accounting practices under Indian Accounting Standards,
                whereas the Commission is determining tariff under GAAP. The said
                expenditure of Rs 2.98 Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall under
                Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. It is also observed that
                petitioner is unable to justify its claim of additional expenditure under the
                Regulations, 2020. Hence, this expenditure of Rs 2.98 Crore is not allowed
                in this Order.

        law. The Commission observed that the aforesaid additional capitalization does
        not fall under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020 i.e., “change in law
        or compliance of any existing law”, hence, additional capitalization of Rs. 0.01
        Crore towards other transformers of power house is not allowed in this Order.

        Petitioner’s Submission

        The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.599 is procurement of :
           a) 80 Nos Ceiling Fan with Regulators amounting to Rs. 0.01 Crores vide

        The above capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 27.2(c) of MPERC

        is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology”.
           b) Portable type fire extinguisher vide order No. 509 dated 01.09.2022
              amounting to Rs. 0.01 Crores.
        CEA has released the Safety Advisory to all Thermal Power Generating Utilities
        on dated 23.05.2022.
        The above capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(d) of MPERC
        Regulations, 2020 which provides for “Any capital expenditure to be incurred on
        account of need for higher security and safety of the plant as advised or directed
        by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory authorities responsible for
        national security/ internal security”.
           c) 4 no. dol starter with relay and 6 no. dol starter for 3HP motor vide
              Document No. 680533 dated 31.03.2023 amounting to Rs. 0.006 Crores.
        The said capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 27.2(c) of MPERC Regulations,
        2020 which provides for “replacement of such asset or equipment is necessary
        on account of obsolescence of technology.

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed Rs. 0.01 Crore under additional capitalization
        towards fire protection system. The said expenditure of Rs. 0.01 Crore claimed
        by the petitioner falls under Regulation 28.1 (d) of the Regulations, 2020 which
        provides for any capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher
        security and safety of the plant. Since, the aforesaid capitalization is done in
        compliance to CEA Advisory dated 23.5.2022 regarding safety advisory to all
        thermal power generating utilities, hence, the aforesaid additional capitalization
        of Rs. 0.01 Crore is allowed in this Order.

        Further, the petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs. 0.006 Crore
        towards dol starters for motor under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations,
        2020 which provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement of
        assets or equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology.
        Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed
        under the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional
        capitalization may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary
        adjustments in the gross fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject
        to prudence check.”

        In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 77
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

             expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the aforesaid
             Regulation 27.2 (c) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the
             replacement of existing assets towards obsolescence of technology. The
             petitioner has also not justified/established any adjustments in the gross fixed
             assets and the cumulative depreciation towards replaced existing assets in its
             Asset-cum-Depreciation register as well as its Annual Audited Accounts. Hence,
             expenditure towards dol starters for motor is not allowed in this Order.

        The Statutory compliance of Indian Accounting Standards (INDAS) is mandatory on
        MPPGCL. Based on said standards, the Expenditure on Major inspection/capital
        Overhaul-Turbine/generator was capitalized in the Audited books of Accounts of
        MPPGCL in FY 2022-23 at Bansagar PH-I, II and III HPS under Account Code 11.603
        amounting to Rs. 25.18 Crores. The same is claimed in the instant petition.

        Commission’s Analysis:
        The petitioner has claimed additional capitalization of Rs. 25.18 Crore towards
        Boiler/Turbine Capital Overhaul Expenditure under Regulation 28.1 (b) which
        provides for “change in law or compliance of any existing law”. It is observed that
        the aforesaid additional capitalization is only book entry due to change in accounting
        practices under Indian Accounting Standards, whereas, the Commission is
        determining tariff under GAAP. The said expenditure of Rs. 25.18 Crore claimed by
        the petitioner does not fall under Regulation 28.1 (b) of the Regulations, 2020. It is
        also observed that petitioner is unable to justify its claim of additional expenditure
        under the Regulations, 2020. Hence, this expenditure of Rs. 25.18 Crore is not
        allowed in this Order at this stage.

     normative loan as per Regulations, 2020, therefore, normative debt equity ratio of 70:30
     is allowed as per the Regulations, 2020.

116. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023, the petitioner was asked to file
     certain details/ documents regarding the additional capitalization in Bansagar HPS (PH-
     4). By affidavit dated 25th January, 2024 the petitioner filed its response to the queries
     raised by the Commission The petitioner has broadly submitted the following:

      i.       Batteries Including Charging Equipments :-
               Petitioner’s Submission
               The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.563 amounting to Rs. 0.59
               Crore is Design, Manufacture, Supply, Erection, Testing & Commissioning of
               “Exide” make tubular battery sets of various rating vide order No. 1904 dated
               29.07.2020.
               This capitalization is carried out in compliance of directives of CEA (Technical
               Standards for Construction of Electrical Plants and Electric Lines), Regulations,
               2010 reproduced as under:-

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                  Page 88
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

             “(6) DC supply system-
             (b) The battery shall have sufficient capacity to meet unit and station
             loads in addition to 3 hours of uninterrupted emergency illumination
             requirement.
             (c) DC system shall comprise of two DC battery sets (both battery
             sets of full capacity) each with one float cum boost charger.
             (d) The float cum boost battery charger as well as its automatic
             regulator shall be of static type. It shall have a facility of both auto
             as well as manual control in both the float and boost modes.”

             The said capitalization is claimed as per Proviso 28.1(b) of MPERC
             Regulations,2020 which provides for “Change in law or compliance of any
             existing law.

             Commission’s Analysis:
             The petitioner has claimed Rs. 0.59 Crore under additional capitalization towards
             batteries including charging equipments of power house under Regulation 28.1 (b)
             of Regulations, 2020 which provides for additional capital expenditure beyond
             original scope of work on account of Change in law or compliance of any existing
             law. The petitioner submitted that the aforesaid capitalization has been done
             towards compliance of CEA (Technical Standards for Construction of Electrical
             Plants and Electric Lines) Regulations, 2010. The said expenditure of Rs. 0.59
             Crore claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the Regulation 28.1 (b) of the
             Regulations, 2020, as the petitioner has not demonstrated any change in law
             event or compliance with existing law in relation to aforesaid CEA Regulations,
             hence, additional capitalization of Rs. 0.59 Crore towards batteries including
             charging equipments is not allowed in this Order at this stage.

117. In view of the above, the Commission has not allowed any additional capitalization in
     Bansagar HPS (PH-4).

        IEGC and MPEGC towards safety/Protection of Generator and Electrical Grid

        & Control (which is beyond original scope of work) is allowed in this Order.

                                              Total                               0.03


126. Vide Commission’s letter dated 28th December, 2023, the petitioner was asked to file
     certain details/ documents regarding the additional capitalization in Madhikheda HPS.
     By affidavit dated 25th January, 2024 the petitioner filed its response to the queries raised
     by the Commission The petitioner has broadly submitted the following:

    i.    Other Office Equipments:-
         The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.904 amounting to Rs. 0.01 Crores
         is

         a) Procurement of 360 no. video cable, 1 no. power supply module, 5 No. DC
                   connector for CCTV camera, 5 no. BNC connector, 2 no. Dome camera, 2 no. bullet
                   camera, 1000 no. HF coaxial cable vides document No. 630005.

                                                              and

         b) Procurement of 2 no. power supply module, 5 no. DC connector for CCTV camera,
                   5 no. BNC connector for CCTV camera,2 no. bullet IP based camera vide document
                   No. 630003.

              Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 28.1(d) which provided for “Any
              capital expenditure to be incurred on account of need for higher security and safety


     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                      Page 91
                                                        MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

           of the plant as advised or directed by appropriate Government Agencies or statutory
           authorities responsible for national security / internal security”

           c) Procurement of 6 No. Air coolers & 2 No. Pedestal fans vide document No. 680139
           dated 30.06.2022.
           Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso and 27.2(c) of MPERC Regulations
           2020, which provides for “The replacement of such asset or equipment is necessary
           on account of obsolescence of technology”.

   ii.    Computers:-
          The Capitalization booked under Account Code 10.905 amounting to Rs. 0.02 Crores
          is :
          a) Procurement of 2 No. Laptops vide order No. 963 dated 03.01.2023
          b) Procurement of Desktop Computer with Laser printers & UPS vide order No. 1433
          dated 31.03.2022.
           Aforesaid capitalization is claimed under proviso 27.2(c) of the Regulations 2020,
           which provides for “The replacement of such asset or equipment is necessary on
           account of obsolescence of technology”
         Commission’s Analysis

         The petitioner has claimed Rs. 0.01 Crore under additional capitalization towards office
         equipments and Rs. 0.02 Crore towards computers under Regulation 27.2 (c) of the
         Regulations, 2020 which provides that the additional capitalization towards replacement
         of assets or equipment is necessary on account of obsolescence of technology.
         Regulation 27.2 further provides that “in case of replacement of assets deployed under
         the original scope of the existing project after cut-off date, the additional capitalization
         may be admitted by the Commission after making necessary adjustments in the gross
         fixed assets and the cumulative depreciation, subject to prudence check.”

         In view of the above provision under the Regulations, it is observed that the said
         expenditure claimed by the petitioner does not fall under the aforesaid Regulation 27.2
         (c) of the Regulations, 2020 as the petitioner has not shown the justification of
         replacement of existing assets towards obsolescence of technology. The Commission
         is of the view that the additional capital expenditure (ACE) incurred by the petitioner
         towards Office equipments & computers is in the nature of Operation & Maintenance
         Expenses. Hence, expenditure of Rs. 0.01 Crore towards office equipments and Rs.
         0.02 Crore towards computers is not allowed in this Order.

         Further, petitioner has not demonstrated/justified the aforementioned expenditure to be
         considered under Regulation 28.1 (d) of the Regulations, 2020, hence not allowed in
         this Order.
127. In view of the above, the Commission has not allowed any additional capitalization under

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                              Page 92
                                                     MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

     the Regulations, 2020 in Madhikheda HPS.

131. With regard to de-capitalization, Regulation 28.2 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that:

            “28.2 In case of de-capitalisation of assets of a generating company, the original cost
            of such asset as on the date of de- capitalisation shall be deducted from the value of
            gross fixed asset and corresponding loan as well as equity shall be deducted from
            outstanding loan and the equity respectively in the year such de-capitalisation takes
            place with corresponding adjustments in cumulative depreciation and cumulative
            repayment of loan, duly taking into consideration the year in which it was capitalised.”

132. On perusal of the power Station/House wise Asset-cum-Depreciation registers filed by
     the petitioner, it is observed that the petitioner has adjusted assets write-off in the power
     stations during the year and then worked out the closing GFA. After considering the same
     approach as considered by the petitioner in its Asset-cum-Depreciation register, the
     Commission has adjusted asset’s write-off during the year and then worked out the
     closing GFA in this Order. The write-off/de-capitalization of assets in different power
     stations is discussed separately as follows:

     Debt-Equity Ratio:

153. With regard to Debt: equity ratio, Regulation 33 of the Regulations, 2020 provides as
     under:

     33.1 For new projects, the debt-equity ratio of 70:30 as on date of commercial operation
          shall be considered. If the equity actually deployed is more than 30% of the capital
          cost, equity in excess of 30% shall be treated as normative loan:
          Provided that:
          a. where equity actually deployed is less than 30% of the capital cost, actual equity
          shall be considered for determination of tariff:
          b. the equity invested in foreign currency shall be designated in Indian rupees on
          the date of each investment:
          c. any grant obtained for the execution of the project shall not be considered as a
          part of capital structure for the purpose of debt : equity ratio.
               Explanation.-The premium, if any, raised by the generating company while
              issuing share capital and investment of internal resources created out of its free
              reserve, for the funding of the project, shall be reckoned as paid up capital for
              the purpose of computing return on equity, only if such premium amount and
              internal resources are actually utilised for meeting the capital expenditure of the
              generating station.
     33.2 The generating company shall submit the resolution of the Board of the company
          regarding infusion of fund from internal resources in support of the utilization made
          or proposed to be made to meet the capital expenditure of the generating station.
     33.3 In case of the generating station declared under commercial operation prior to
          1.4.2019, debt- equity ratio allowed by the Commission for determination of tariff
          for the period ending 31.3.2019 shall be considered:
                  Provided that in case of a generating station which has completed its useful
          life as on or after 01.04.2019, if the equity actually deployed as on 01.04.2019 is
          more than 30% of the capital cost, equity in excess of 30% shall not be taken into
          account for tariff determination.
     33.4 In case of the generating station declared under commercial operation prior to
          1.4.2019, but where debt: equity ratio has not been determined by the Commission
          for determination of tariff for the period ending 31.3.2019, the Commission shall
          approve the debt : equity in accordance with Regulation 33.1 of these Regulations.
     33.5 Any expenditure incurred or projected to be incurred on or after 1.4.2019 as may be
          admitted by the Commission as additional capital expenditure for determination of
          tariff, and renovation and modernisation expenditure for life extension shall be

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 104
                                                     MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

            serviced in the manner specified in clause 33.1 of this Regulation

154. Based on above Regulation, the Commission has considered power station wise opening
     loan and equity as on 01st April, 2022 same as closing loan and equity balances as on
     31st March, 2022 considered in last true-up Order for FY 2021-22 issued on 31st March,
     2023.

155. Further, Regulation 33.5 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that any expenditure incurred
     on or after 01st April, 2019 as may be admitted by the Commission as additional capital
     expenditure for determination of tariff shall be determined in the manner specified in
     Regulations 33.1 of this Regulation. Therefore, the debt and equity component for the
     additional capitalization admitted in this Order has been considered accordingly.

     Annual Capacity (fixed) Charges:

156. Regulation 17 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that Annual Capacity (fixed) Charges
     derived on the basis of annual fixed cost (AFC) which shall consist of the following
     components:

             (a)     Return on Equity;
             (b)     Depreciation
             (c)     Interest on Loan Capital;
             (d)     Operation and Maintenance Expenses
             (e)     Interest on Working Capital;

     Provision in Regulations:
159. With Regards to Return on Equity, Regulation 34 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that:

     34 . Return on Equity:
     34.1 Return on equity shall be computed in rupee terms on the equity base determined
             in accordance with Regulation 33 of these Regulations.
     34.2 Return on equity shall be computed at the base rate of 15.50% for thermal
             generating stations and hydro generating stations and at the base rate of 16.50%
             for the pumped storage hydro generating stations and run-of river generating
             stations with pondage.
            Provided that
          i.       in case of a new project, the rate of return of a new project shall be reduced
                   by 1.00% for such period as may be decided by the Commission, if the
                   generating station is found to be declared under commercial operation without
                   commissioning of any of the Restricted Governor Mode Operation (RGMO)/
                   Free Governor Mode Operation (FGMO):
         ii.       in case of existing generating station any of the above requirements are found
                   lacking based on the report submitted by the respective SLDC/RLDC, RoE
                   shall be reduced by 1% for the period for which the deficiency continues.
        iii.       in case of a thermal generating station, with effect from 1.04.2020:
             (a) rate of return on equity shall be reduced by 0.25% in case of failure to achieve
                   the ramp rate of 1% per minute:
             (b) an additional rate of return on equity of 0.25% shall be allowed for every
                   incremental ramp rate of 1% per minute achieved over and above the ramp
                   rate of 1% per minute, subject to ceiling of additional rate of return on equity
                   of 1.00%:

     Commission’s Analysis:

160. In this Order, the Commission has considered the power station wise opening equity as
     on 1st April, 2022 as the closing equity considered in last true-up order for FY 2021-22

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                Page 107
                                                       MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

     issued on 31st March, 2023.

161. With regard to ROE in respect of additional capitalization, after the cut-off date beyond
     the original scope of work, proviso of Regulation 34.2 of the 2 nd Amendment to the
     Regulations ,2020 provides that:

            “Provided also that return on equity in respect of additional capitalization after cutoff
            date beyond the original scope of work, excluding additional capitalization on
            account of emission control system, shall be computed at the weighted average
            rate of interest on actual loan portfolio of the generating station or in the absence of
            actual loan portfolio of the generating station, the weighted average rate of interest
            of the generating company ,as a whole shall be considered , subject to ceiling of
            14%”

162. The petitioner filed the additional capitalization in the thermal and hydel power stations
     during FY 2022-23 and claimed return on equity on additional equity infusion in respect
     of additional capitalization. The Commission has considered the power station wise
     equity addition within original scope of work and beyond original scope of work only to
     the extent of additional capitalization considered in this true-up Order. The equity over
     and above the normative equity is considered as normative loan. The Commission has
     also considered the reduction of equity amount in respect of the asset deletion/de-
     capitalization in respective power stations during the year.

     Provision in Regulations:
169. With regard to Depreciation, Regulation 37 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that:

     “37.1 “Depreciation shall be computed from the date of commercial operation of a
          generating station or unit thereof. In case of the tariff of all the units of a generating
          station for which a single tariff needs to be determined, the depreciation shall be
          computed from the effective date of commercial operation of the generating station
          taking into consideration the depreciation of individual units:
              Provided that effective date of commercial operation shall be worked out by
             considering the actual date of commercial operation and installed capacity of all
             the units of the generating station for which single tariff needs to be determined.

     37.2 The value base for the purpose of depreciation shall be the capital cost of the asset
          admitted by the Commission. In case of multiple units of a generating station,
          weighted average life for the generating station shall be applied.

     37.3 The salvage value of the asset shall be considered as 10% and depreciation shall
          be allowed up to maximum of 90% of the capital cost of the asset:
             Provided that in case of hydro generating station, the salvage value shall be as
            provided in the agreement, if any, signed by the developers with the State
            Government for development of the generating station:

              Provided further that the capital cost of the assets of the hydro generating station
              for the purpose of computation of depreciated value shall correspond to the
              percentage of sale of electricity under long-term power purchase agreement at
              regulated tariff:

               Provided also that any depreciation disallowed on account of lower availability
              of the generating station or generating unit shall not be allowed to be recovered
              at a later stage during the useful life or the extended life:

               Provided also that the salvage value for IT equipment and software shall be
              considered as NIL and 100% value of the assets shall be considered
              depreciable.

     37.4 Land other than the land held under lease and the land for reservoir in case of hydro
          generating station shall not be a depreciable asset and its cost shall be excluded
          from the capital cost while computing depreciable value of the asset.
     37.5 Depreciation shall be calculated annually based on ‘Straight Line Method’ and at
          rates specified in Appendix-Ito these Regulations for the assets of the generating
          station.
     37.6 Depreciation shall be chargeable from the first Year of commercial operation. In

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 113
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

           case of commercial operation of the asset for part of the Year, depreciation shall be
           charged on pro rata basis:

               Provided that the remaining depreciable value as on 31st March of the year
              closing after a period of 12 years from the effective date of commercial operation
              of the station shall be spread over the balance useful life of the assets.

     37.7 In case of the existing projects, the balance depreciable value as on 1.4.2019 shall
          be worked out by deducting the cumulative depreciation as admitted by the
          Commission upto 31.3.2019 from the gross depreciable value of the assets.

     37.8 The generating company shall submit the details of proposed capital expenditure
          five years before the completion of useful life of the project along with justification
          and proposed life extension. The Commission based on prudence check of such
          submissions shall approve the depreciation on capital expenditure during the fag
          end of the project.

     37.9 In case of de-capitalization of assets in respect of generating station or unit thereof,
           the cumulative depreciation shall be adjusted by taking into account the
           depreciation recovered in tariff by the de-capitalized asset during its useful
           services.”

     Commission’s Analysis:
170. Regulation 37.2 specified that the value base for the purpose of depreciation shall be the
     capital cost of the asset admitted by the Commission. In accordance with the aforesaid
     Regulation, Gross Fixed Assets as on 31st March, 2022 admitted by the Commission in
     the last True-up order for FY 2021-22 issued on 31st March, 2023 in petition No. 85 of
     2022 is considered as the base opening figure of capital cost as on 01 st April, 2022 for all
     power stations.

171. The petitioner mentioned that in case of asset addition made during the year, the
     depreciation is charged on pro-rata basis, based on the commercial operation of the
     assets for part of the year.

172. As per proviso 37.6 of the Regulations 2020 which provides that the remaining
     depreciable value as on 31st March of the year closing after a period of 12 years from the
     effective date of commercial operation of the station and shall be spread over the balance
     useful life of the assets. The petitioner submitted that it has already modified their Asset-
     cum-Depreciation registers in accordance with the aforesaid Regulations.

173. By affidavit dated 25th January, 2024, the petitioner has filed Assets-cum-Depreciation
     Register. The Commission has considered Weighted Average rate of Depreciation rates
     as submitted by the petitioner of FY 2022-23 for all power stations.
     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 114
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023



174. The Commission observed that ATPS PH-3, SGTPS PH-1&2, SGTPS PH-3 and all Hydro
     power stations have completed useful life of 12 years. Accordingly, balance depreciation
     of these power stations has been spread over the balance useful life of the assets.

175. Further, cumulative depreciation of STPS PH 2&3 already reaches to 90% of the GFA
     therefore, no depreciation is considered in this power station.

176. Accumulated Depreciation balances (as on 31 st March, 2022) admitted by the
     Commission in last true-up order for FY 2021-22 for all power stations is considered as
     the base opening figures as on 01st April, 2022. Further, adjustment in Accumulated
     Depreciation towards de-capitalized assets is also considered to work out Adjusted
     Opening Accumulated Depreciation.

177. Based on the above, the opening GFA, additions admitted during the year, deductions
     towards de-capitalised assets considered during the year and the closing GFA is worked
     out as given below:

      Table 71: Power Station wise Opening & Closing GFA Balances worked out in this Order

     Provision in Regulations:
182. Regulation 36 of the Regulations 2020, provides that:

     36.1 The loans arrived at in the manner indicated in Regulation 33 of these Regulations

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                                 Page 118
                                                      MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

           shall be considered as gross normative loan for calculation of interest on loan.
     36.2 The normative loan outstanding as on 1.4.2019 shall be worked out by deducting
          the cumulative repayment as admitted by the Commission up to 31.3.2019 from the
           gross normative loan. The repayment for each of the year of the tariff period 2019-
           24 shall be deemed to be equal to the depreciation allowed for the corresponding
           year/period. In case of de- capitalization of assets, the repayment shall be adjusted
           by taking into account cumulative repayment on a pro rata basis and the adjustment
           should not exceed cumulative depreciation recovered upto the date of de-
           capitalisation of such asset
     36.3 Notwithstanding any moratorium period availed by the generating company, the
          repayment of loan shall be considered from the first year of commercial operation
           of the project and shall be equal to the depreciation allowed for the year or part of
           the year.
     36.4 The rate of interest shall be the weighted average rate of interest calculated on the
          basis of the actual loan portfolio after providing appropriate accounting adjustment
           for interest capitalized:
                  Provided that if there is no actual loan for a particular year but normative loan
           is still outstanding, the last available weighted average rate of interest shall be
           considered:
                  Provided further that if the generating station does not have actual loan, then
           the weighted average rate of interest of the generating company as a whole shall
           be considered.
     36.5 The interest on loan shall be calculated on the normative average loan of the year
          by applying the weighted average rate of interest.

     Commission’s analysis:

     April, 2022 as per the closing loan considered in last true-up order for FY 2021-22 issued
     on 31st March, 2023.

     summarised in preceding paras to the extent of additional capitalization is considered in
     this Order. The Commission has also considered the reduction of loan amount in respect
     of the assets deletion/de-capitalized in respective power stations during the year.

     Provision in Regulations:
193. Regarding the operation and maintenance expenses of thermal power stations,
     Regulation 40.1 & 40.2 of Regulations, 2020, provides as under:

     40.1“The Operation and Maintenance expenses admissible to the thermal power
          stations declared under commercial operation prior to 01.04.2012 comprise of
          employee cost, Repair & Maintenance (R&M) cost and Administrative and General
          (A&G) cost. These norms for O&M exclude Pension and Terminal Benefits, EL
          encashment, and arrears to be paid to employees, taxes payable to the
          Government, and fees payable to MPERC. The generating company shall claim
          the rate, rent & taxes payable to the Government, cost of chemicals and
          consumables, fees to be paid to MPERC, EL encashment and any arrears paid to
          employees separately as actual. If the O&M expenses as per norms provided in
          these Regulations are more than the actual total O&M expenses including arrears
          if any, as per audited accounts of generating company, the O&M expenses to the
          extent of normative O&M expenses shall be allowed. The claim of pension and
          Terminal Benefits shall be dealt as per Regulation 39.4 of these Regulations.”

e)   Special allowance:
     Petitioner’s submission

197. With regard to the special allowance, the petitioner submitted the following:
            “The proviso 30 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that the Generating Company,
             in case of thermal generating stations, instead of availing R&M may opt to avail a
             “special allowance” in accordance with the norms specified in this Regulation, as
             compensation for meeting the requirement of expenses including renovation and
             modernization beyond the useful life of the generating station or a unit thereof,
             and in such an event, upward revision of the capital cost shall not be allowed and
             the applicable operational norms shall not be relaxed but the special allowance
             shall be included in the annual fixed cost.
             The Commission vide MYT order dated 19.05.2021 in petition No. 53 of 2020 has
             permitted Special Allowance for Units of PH-2 & 3 STPS, Sarni upto FY 2019-20
             to FY 2023-24. Accordingly, the Truing up of the same has been considered in the
             petition.

     Provision in the Regulations:
198. With regard to special allowance, Regulation 30.2 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that:

           “The special allowance admissible to the generating station shall be @ Rs. 9.5
           lakh/MW/year for the tariff period 2019-24.”
     Commission’s Analysis:
199. Regulation 30 of the Regulations, 2020 provides the special allowance for coal based
     thermal power stations instead of availing R&M for meeting the requirement of expenses
     including R&M beyond the useful life of generating station. Proviso of Regulations 30.2
     of the Regulations 2020 further provides that the special allowance admissible to the
     generating station shall be @ Rs. 9.5 lakh/MW/year for the tariff period 2019-24.

200. The Commission vide MYT order dated 19th May, 2021 considered the special allowance
     for Unit No 6, 7, 8 & 9 of STPS, Sarni PH-2&3. In view of the above, the Commission has
     considered the special allowance for FY 2022-23 for the units of STPS PH-2&3 as given
     below:

     Provision in Regulations:
203. regarding working capital for coal based generating stations, Regulation 38.1 (A) of the
     Regulations, 2020 provides that:

     38.1 “The Working Capital shall cover:
     A. Coal- based thermal generating stations
        i. Cost of coal towards stock, if applicable, for 15 days for pit-head generating stations
           and 30 days for non-pit-head generating stations for generation corresponding to
           the normative annual plant availability factor or the maximum coal stock storage
           capacity whichever is lower;
       ii. Advance payment for 30 days towards cost of coal for generation corresponding to
           the normative annual plant availability factor;
      iii. Cost of secondary fuel oil for two months for generation corresponding to the
           normative annual plant availability factor, and in case of use of more than one
           secondary fuel oil, cost of fuel oil stock for the main secondary fuel oil;
      iv. Maintenance spares @ 20% of operation and maintenance expenses specified in
           Regulation 39 and 40 of these Regulations;
       v. Receivables equivalent to 45 days of capacity charges and energy charges for sale
           of electricity calculated on the normative annual plant availability factor; and
      vi. Operation and maintenance expenses for one month.

204. Regarding working capital for hydel power stations, Regulation 38.1 (B) of the
     Regulations, 2020 provides that:

     B.      “Hydro Generating Station, the Working Capital shall include:
          (i) Receivables equivalent to 45 days of Annual fixed cost;

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 127
                                                        MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

           (ii) Maintenance spares @ 15% of operation and maintenance expenses specified in
                 Regulation 39 and 41 of these Regulations; and
           (iii) Operation and maintenance expenses for one month

205. With regard to interest rate on working capital, Regulation 38.3 of the Regulations, 2020
     provides that:

            “Rate of interest on working capital shall be on normative basis and shall be
            considered as the bank rate1.4.2019 or as on 1st April of the year during the tariff
            period 2019-20 to 2023-24 in which the generating station or a unit thereof, is
            declared under commercial operation, whichever is later:

                       Provided that in case of truing-up, the rate of interest on working capital
            shall be considered at bank rate as on 1st April of each of the financial year during
            the tariff period 2019-24.”

     Provision under Regulations
209. With Regard to Non-Tariff Income, Regulation 58.1 of the Regulations, 2020 provides
     that:

      58.1 “The non-tariff net income in case of generating station on account of following
          shall be shared in the ratio of 50:50 with the beneficiaries and the generating
          company on annual basis:
      a) Income from rent of land or buildings;
      b) Income from sale of scrap;
      c) Income from sale of fly ash;
      d) Interest on advances to suppliers or contractors;

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 132
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

      e)    Rental from staff quarters;
      f)    Rental from contractors;
      g)    Income from advertisements; and
      h)    Interest on investments and bank balances:

             Provided that the interest or dividend earned from investments made out of Return
            on Equity corresponding to the regulated business of the Generating Company shall
            not be included in Non-Tariff Income:

             Provided further that the Generation Company shall submit full details of its
            forecast of Non-Tariff Income to the Commission. Non-tariff income shall also be
            trued-up based on audited accounts.

     Commission’s Analysis:

     2022-23. The petitioner was also asked to explain the reasons for any difference in
     figures recorded in Annual Audited Accounts and filed in the subject petition.

             2020
