    The Power Station wise Other Income as per the audited books of accounts for
    FY 2022-23 as reflected in Note -26 amounts to Rs. 523.97 Crores.
    The aforesaid Other Income amounting to Rs.523.7 Crores includes following
    income which has not been considered under the head Non–Tariff income in
    accordance with MPERC Regulations.

(a) Surcharge due to delayed payment by MPPMCL.
    MPPGCL has levied the surcharge on MPPMCL on delay in payment of Bills in
    accordance with proviso 55.1 of the Regulations, 2020 read with PPA’s entered with
    MPPMCL. The amount of surcharge worked out to Rs. 203.86 Crore.
    The proviso 58.1 of the Regulations 2020 does not include/specifies the income from
    Surcharge due to delay in payment of Energy bills as part of Non-Tariff Income.
    Accordingly, MPPGCL humbly request Commission not to consider the Income from
    Surcharge due to delayed payment by MPPMCL as part of Non-Tariff Income.
    It is to submit that the Commission in the past True Up Orders has not considered
    surcharge on delayed payments as Non-Tariff Income. The said approach may
    kindly be considered.

(b) Interest Income on Security Deposit due to Unwinding of Discount:
    MPPGCL has adopted Indian Accounting Standard (INDAS) from FY 2016-17
    onwards. The said Accounting Standard provides for recognition of notional income
    on account of expected gain considering discounting of Interest on Security Deposit.
    Accordingly, in compliance to INDAS, MPPGCL has recognized notional income of
    Rs. 0.91 Crores at Power Stations in the Financial Statement of Accounts for FY
    2022-23.
    Here it is to mention that this amount of Rs. 0.91 Crore is merely a Book adjustment,
    and no actual cash is received by MPPGCL. It is to mention that the Commission in
    the True Up order for FY 2017-18 to FY 2021-22 has not considered this head for
    determination of Non-Tariff income.
    MPPGCL, therefore requests before Commission to not to consider this- amount of
    Rs. 0.91 Crores as part of Non-Tariff income.


(c) Income from O&M Contracts –

    O&M of plant & Equipment of POWERGRID (PGCIL) at SGTPS, Birsinghpur and
    STPS, Sarni.


M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 134
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

     MPPGCL has entered into contract with POWERGRID (PGCIL) for O&M of Plant &
     Equipments at Substations at SGTPS, Birsinghpur and STPS, Sarni.
     As per the requirement of financial accounts the amount received / receivable
     towards O&M charges is to be treated as Other Income in Audited Books of Accounts
     of MPPGCL. This amounts to total Rs. 0.46 Crore.
     As this receivable of Rs. 0.46 Crores is a sort of reimbursement towards expenditure
     incurred by MPPGCL for FY 2022-23, therefore, the same may please not be
     considered as part of Non- Tariff income.
     It is to mention that the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2017-18 to FY 2021-
     22 has not considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.
     The Total Income from O&M Contracts (STPS & SGTPS) is Rs. 0.46 Crore is a sort
     of reimbursement towards expenditure incurred by MPPGCL, therefore, the same
     may kindly not to be considered as part of Non- Tariff income.

 (d) Income from Grant.
     The MPERC Regulations, 2020 at proviso 15.6 provides as under:-
“21.5 The following shall be excluded or removed from the capital cost of the
     existing and new projects:

           ………..
   (e) any grant received from the Central or State Government or any
     statutory body or authority for the execution of the project which
     does not carry any liability of repayment shall be excluded from the
     Capital Cost for the purpose of computation of interest on loan, return on
     equity and depreciation;
     Accordingly, Commission in the True Up Tariff petition earlier years has not
     permitted the additional capital expenditure towards assets whose funding was met
     from Grant.
     Further, MPPGCL has adopted Indian Accounting Standard (INDAS) from FY 2016-
     17 onwards. The said Accounting Standard provides for recognition of income as
     adjustment towards treatment of grant received. Accordingly, MPPGCL has
     recognized notional income of total Rs. 7.97 Crore at Note 26.1 (Other Income) in
     the Financial Statement of Accounts for FY 2022-23, which is merely a Book
     adjustment.
     It is to mention that the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2018-19 to FY 2021-
     22 has not considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.
     Therefore, based on above submission, MPPGCL request Commission not to
     consider the Income from Grant amounting to Rs. 7.97 Crore as part of Non-Tariff
     Income.

 (e) Reversal of Excess Pension & Gratuity provision :
 M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 135
                                              MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

    MPPGCL has adopted Indian Accounting Standard (INDAS) from FY 2016-17. The
    said Accounting Standards read with Companies Act 1956 provides for recognition
    of provisions and reserves based on prescribed procedure. Accordingly, based on
    Actual Valuation Report, provision was created in the Books of Accounts for Pension
    & Gratuity in previous years. As of now the Terminal Liabilities towards Pension and
    Gratuity liabilities are borne by MPPTCL and same are claimed by them in their
    Tariff.
    Based on Actual Valuation got conducted by MPPGCL in FY 2022-23, it was found
    that excess provision was created in the Books of Accounts of MPPGCL for erstwhile
    MPSEB Cadre employees .Accordingly, the amount of Rs.55.08 Crores towards
    pension provision & Rs.9.99 Crores towards Gratuity Provision was reversed and
    reflected in Note 26.1 (Other Income) of Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2022-23
    being adjustment book entry for excess provision made in earlier years.
    MPPGCL request Commission not consider the aforesaid Reversal of excess
    provisions as part of Non-Tariff Income being an adjustment book entry.

(f) Other Income from RP Sagar, Jawahar Sagar HPS :-

    The Rana Pratap Sagar HPS and Jawahar Sagar HPS are situated in Rajasthan and
    are being operated by Rajasthan Rajya Vidyut Prasaran Nigam Ltd. The Commission
    does not determine the Tariff of these Hydro Stations, accordingly the income from
    these Hydro Power Stations has not been considered as Non-Tariff Income for tariff
    purpose. The total amount on account of above is Rs. 0.01 Crore.
    Further there is Misc. Receipts at Headquarters amounting to Rs. 0.04 Crores whose
    Tariff is not determined by Commission, accordingly the income at Headquarters has
    not been considered as Non-Tariff Income for tariff purpose.

(g) Reversal of Excess Asset Retirement Obligation Provision:
    MPPGCL has adopted Indian Accounting Standard (INDAS) from FY 2016-17. As
    said Indian Accounting Standards, an Asset retirement obligation (ARO) is a legal or
    contractual obligation to dismantle and remove an asset and to restore the site in
    which it is located on retirement of a tangible asset. The company estimates its
    obligation for assets retirement based on technical assessment and timing of future
    expenditure for required work considering the inflation factor and discounting rate.
    Accordingly, provision towards Asset Retirement obligation was created in the books
    in the previous years.
    In FY 2022-23, the Asset retirement obligation (ARO) estimates were revised and
    accordingly the excess provision was reversed in in the Books of Accounts of
    MPPGCL amounting to Rs.17.93 Crore as reflected in Note 26.1 (Other Income) of


M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 136
                                                MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

    Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2021-22 being adjustment book entry for excess
    provision made in earlier years.
    MPPGCL request Commission not consider the aforesaid Reversal of excess
    provision amounting to Rs. 17.93 Crore as part of Non-Tariff Income being an
    adjustment book entry.

(h) Interest on Fixed Deposit on R&M and e-Genco Loans.

    MPPGCL keeps the GoMP R&M Loan Fund as Flexi Fixed Deposit (FFD) amounting
    to Rs. 127 Crores approx. in the Union Bank of India and makes payment through
    this fund under R&M Scheme. This R&M fund also include Rs. 78 Crore for ERP
    implementation.
    In the FY 2022-23, MPPGCL received/receivable Interest on FFD Rs.1.99 Crores
    from Union Bank of India on R&M Fund / e-Genco fund balance with Union Bank of
    India. The ERP implementation is under progress at MPPGCL. Further the works
    proposed through GoMP R&M Loan are mainly for compliance of statutory
    guidelines (Grid Code /CEA Regulations etc.) and need based schemes essential
    for efficient running of units.
    It is to mention that the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2017-18 to FY 2021-
    22 has not considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.
    MPPGCL request Commission not consider the Interest on above fixed deposits Rs.
    0.99 Crore as part of Non-Tariff Income.

(i) Reversal of Ex-gratia provision.
    The Payment of Bonus Act, 1965 read with subsequent amendments provide for the
    payment of Bonus/ Ex-gratia to persons employed in certain establishments on the
    basis of profits or on the basis of production or productivity and for matters connected
    therewith. In compliance to the above act the erstwhile MPEB/ MPSEB from time to
    time had issued various orders/ circulars for payment of Bonus/ Ex-gratia to its
    employees.
    This procedure has also been adopted by MPPGCL and accordingly suitable
    provisions are made. Suitable arrangements have also been made by way of
    reversal entry towards the fund remaining un-utilized under the head Bonus/ Ex-
    gratia.
    An amount of Rs.2.83 Crore towards reversal of Ex-gratia provision is embedded in
    Misc. Receipts under Note 26.1 (Other Income) of Audited Books of Accounts for FY
    2022-23. MPPGCL humbly request Commission not consider the Reversal of Ex-
    gratia provision as part of Non-Tariff Income being an adjustment entry.
    It is to mention that the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2018-19 to FY 2021-
    22 has not considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 137
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023


(j) Recovery of liquidated damages at SSTPP Khandwa Stage-I:-

    The unit No.1 & 2(2x600MW) of SSTPP PH-1 was commissioned on 01.02.2014
    and 28.12.2014 respectively. The Commission vide order dated 30.12.2017 in
    petition No. 09 of 2015 has determined the Final Generation Tariff of SSTPP PH-1
    from Cod of respective units to 31.03.2016.
    In the above mentioned Final Tariff order in Petition No. 09 of 2015 at Para 73, Table
    No.9, Page 34 has determined the excess IDC and FC due to delay in
    commissioning amounting to Rs. 565.12 Crore. The Commission has
    deducted/disallowed 50% of excess IDC & FC from capital cost claimed beyond
    schedule CoD of units amounting to Rs.282.56 Crore.
    Here it is to mention that the amount of Liquidated damages to be recovered from
    vendors/contractors on account of delay in completion of project was not finalized at
    the time of determination of Final Generation of unit No.1 & 2(2x600MW) of SSTPP
    PH-1 Khandwa.
    In the above context, it is to submit that the amount of Liquidated damages for delay
    in completion of project was finalized in FY 2022-23 amounting to Rs 168 Crore
    approx. & out of which amount of Rs. 132.02 Crore was recovered in FY 2022-23 as
    reflected in Note-26.1 of Audited Accounts for FY 2022-23.
    In reference to above, proviso 23.5 of MPERC Regulations, 2020 provides as under:
    “If the delay is attributable either in entirety or in part to the generating
    company or its contractor or supplier or agency, in such cases, IDC and
    IEDC beyond SCOD may be disallowed after due prudence check either
    in entirety or on pro-rata basis corresponding to the period of delay not
    condoned and the liquidated damages, if any, recovered from the
    contractor or supplier or agency shall be retained by the generating
    company.”
    As evident from above, the liquidated damages, recovered from the contractor or
    supplier is allowed to be retained by the generating company.
    Further, as stated above, the Commission in the Final Tariff Order for SSTPP PH-I
    Khandwa has deducted/disallowed 50% of excess IDC & FC from capital cost
    claimed beyond schedule CoD of units amounting to Rs.282.56 Crore.
    As per the proviso 58.1 of the Regulations,2020, the non-tariff income in case of
    generating station on account of Other Income shall be shared in the ratio of 50:50
    with the beneficiaries and the generating company on annual basis.
    Considering above, Commission has already provided the benefit the beneficiary
    amounting to Rs.282.56 which is double of LD recovery made by MPPGCL
    amounting to Rs. 132.02 Crore.




M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 138
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

    In light of above, MPPGCL requests before Commission to not to consider this
    amount of Rs. 132.02 Crore towards recovery of Liquidated Damages as part of
    Non-Tariff income.

(k) Income Tax Refund:-

    An amount of Rs. 1.02 Crore towards Income Tax refund was received in FY 2022-
    23 & same is reflected in Note 26.1 (Other Income) of Audited Books of Accounts
    for FY 2022-23.
    The said refund is on account of Tax deducted at Source (TDS), Tax collected at
    source (TCS) on interest etc and same has been refunded on completion of
    assessment proceedings by the tax authorities.
    Considering the set-off of & carry forward losses provisions of per Income Tax Act ,
    there was no tax liability on MPPGCL in previous Financial Years and accordingly
    MPPGCL has not claimed any grossing up of Rate of return on Equity in previous
    Financial Years as per prevailing MPERC Regulations.
    Accordingly, MPPGCL has neither made any recovery in Tariff on account of Income
    tax, nor any grossing up of Rate of return on Equity was claimed, therefore the refund
    of Income tax amounting to Rs.1.02 Crore may kindly not be treated as part of Non-
    Tariff Income as per proviso 58.1 of the Regulations 2020.
    It is to mention that the Commission in the True Up order for FY 2020-21 & FY 2021-
    22 has not considered this head for determination of Non-Tariff income.

(l) Recovery of Ash Transportation Expenses:-

    In compliance to MoEF&CC Notification, MPPGCL has an obligation to transport ash
    produced from its Thermal plants to designated entities and bear the cost of
    transportation. The said cost of Transportation is recoverable from beneficiary
    (MPPMCL) as per guidelines of Commission.
    In this regard, MPPGCL has incurred expenditure of Rs.11.56 Crore towards
    Transportation of Fly Ash during FY 2022-23 as reflected in Note 31.1 at Serial No.8
    of Audited Financial Statements of FY 2022-23. The said amount towards Recovery
    of Ash Transportation Expenses was billed to MPPMCL and reflected in Note 26.1
    (Other Income) of Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2022-23. The supporting
    documents in this regard were submitted before Commission vide letter No.61 dated
    15.01.2024.
    The proviso 58.1 of the Regulations 2020 does not include/specifies Recovery of
    Ash Transportation Expenses as part of Non-Tariff Income. Further, the recovery
    was made as per guidelines of MPERC Accordingly, MPPGCL request Commission
    not to consider the Recovery of Ash Transportation Expenses amounting to Rs.
    11.56 Crore as part of Non-Tariff Income.


M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 139
                                                       MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

     (m) Coal Penalty:-

          In accordance with Fuel Supply Agreement (FSA), Coal is to be transported from
          Coal Mines to the Thermal Power Stations of MPPGCL. Rail-cum-Road (RCR) is
          one of the modes through which coal is transported by Contractors to the Thermal
          Power Stations of MPPGCL. The transportation contract provides for various terms
          and conditions towards Quantity, Loading/uploading, delivery timing etc and various
          penalties are prescribed in contract for non-compliances of said terms and
          conditions.
          Considering above, Coal penalty amounting to Rs.10.85 Crore was recovered in FY
          2022-23 from the contractors and same was reflected in Note 26.1 (Other Income)
          of Audited Books of Accounts for FY 2022-23. It is to mention that said penalties
          was adjusted/recovered against the Bills/invoices raised by the contractors and for
          the point of presentation of Financial Statements as per Companies Act, the amount
          of penalty is shown separately under the Head- Other income. Further proviso 58.1
          of the Regulations 2020 does not include/specifies coal penalties as part of Non-
          Tariff Income.
          Accordingly, MPPGCL request Commission not to consider coal penalties
          amounting to Rs. 10.85 Crore as part of Non-Tariff Income.

          In view of above submission, it is requested before Commission to kindly the Non-
          Tariff Income of Rs. 34.07 Crore as submitted by MPPGCL in the instant petition

212. In view of the above, it is observed that the petitioner has filed the head-wise justifications
     for all components recorded under Operating income but not considered by them under
     non-tariff income.

213. On going through the above-mentioned reason stated by the petitioner for not considering
     liquidated damages recovered in SSTPP Khandwa PH-1 in non-tariff income, Hon’ble
     Appellate Tribunal for Electricity (APTEL) in its Judgment in Appeal No. 72 of 2010 has
     held the following:

     “7.4. The delay in execution of a generating project could occur due to following reasons:
        i) due to factors entirely attributable to the generating company, e.g., imprudence in
        selecting the contractors/suppliers and in executing contractual agreements including
        terms and conditions of the contracts, delay in award of contracts, delay in providing
        inputs like making land available to the contractors, delay in payments to
        contractors/suppliers as per the terms of contract, mismanagement of finances,
        slackness in project management like improper co-ordination between the various
        contractors, etc.
        ii) due to factors beyond the control of the generating company e.g. delay caused due
        to force majeure like natural calamity or any other reasons which clearly establish,

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                             Page 140
                                                        MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

        beyond any doubt, that there has been no imprudence on the part of the generating
        company in executing the project

        iii)situation not covered by (i) & (ii) above. In our opinion in the first case the entire cost
        due to time over run has to be borne by the generating company. However, the
        Liquidated Damages (LDs) and insurance proceeds on account of delay, if any,
        received by the generating company could be retained by the generating company. In
        the second case the generating company could be given benefit of the additional cost
        incurred due to time over-run. However, the consumers should get full benefit of the
        LDs recovered from the contractors/suppliers of the generating company and the
        insurance proceeds, if any, to reduce the capital cost. In the third case the additional
        cost due to time overrun including the LDs and insurance proceeds could be shared
        between the generating company and the consumer. It would also be prudent to
        consider the delay with respect to some benchmarks rather than depending on the
        provisions of the contract between the generating company and its
        contractors/suppliers. If the time schedule is taken as per the terms of the contract,
        this may result in imprudent time schedule not in accordance with good industry
        practices.”

214. With regard to sharing of impact on account of time overrun due to mix of controllable
     and uncontrollable factors, Hon’ble APTEL in its Judgment has ruled as follows:

     “7.12. In view of above, we feel that this case falls under category (iii) described in Para
     7.4. Accordingly, following the principles of prudence check laid down by us, the cost of
     time over run has to be shared equally between the generating company and the
     consumers. Admittedly, there is no enhancement in cost of the contract price of the
     equipment as no price variation escalation was permissible to BHEL beyond the schedule
     date of completion of the Project according to the terms of the agreement. The impact of
     time over run beyond the contractual schedule is only on IDC and overhead costs.
     Accordingly, the same have to be shared between the generating company and the
     consumers. Excess IDC and overhead costs for time overrun from scheduled date of
     commissioning to actual date of commissioning has to be worked out on prorate basis
     with respect to total actual time taken in commissioning of the unit. 50% of the excess
     IDC and overhead costs will have to be disallowed. Deduction on account of 50% of the
     Liquidity Damages received by the Appellant from its suppliers/contractors has also to be
     allowed from the capital cost, to give due credit for LDs to the consumers. This issue is
     answered accordingly.

215. In Commission’s tariff Order of SSTPP PH-1 dated 30th March, 2017 in Petition No 9 of
     2017, the Commission has ruled the following with regards to Liquidated damages:

         62. On perusal of above reply of petitioner, the petitioner is directed to furnish the

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                              Page 141
                                                            MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

          detailed information of actual LD/ penalty as and when deducted from contractor/
          vendor. The aforesaid information be filed with the next petition for true up of SSTPP
          Stage-I, Unit No. 1 and 2.”

216. Accordingly, as per the above direction of the Commission and in line with the above
     Judgment towards “Deduction on account of 50% of the Liquidity Damages received by
     the Appellant from its suppliers/contractors has also to be allowed from the capital cost,
     to give due credit for LDs to the consumers”, the Commission has considered 50% of
     LD damage costs recovery of Rs. 66.01 Crore under non-tariff income of SSTPP
     PH-1.

217. Accordingly, the total non-tariff income of Rs. 100.08 Crore for the power stations in
     accordance with the Regulations, 2020 is allowed in this Order. The break-up of 50%

     Commission’s Analysis:

220. Regarding the other charges, Regulation 40.1 and 41.1 of the Regulations, 2020 provides
     the following:

     “40.1The Operation and Maintenance expenses admissible to the thermal power stations
          declared under commercial operation prior to 01.04.2012 comprise of employee
          cost, Repair & Maintenance (R&M) cost and Administrative and General (A&G)
          cost. These norms for O&M exclude Pension and Terminal Benefits, EL
          encashment, and arrears to be paid to employees, taxes payable to the
          Government, and fees payable to MPERC. The generating company shall claim the
          rate, rent & taxes payable to the Government, cost of chemicals and consumables,
          fees to be paid to MPERC, EL encashment and any arrears paid to employees
          separately as actual. If the O&M expenses as per norms provided in these
          Regulations are more than the actual total O&M expenses including arrears if any,
          as per audited accounts of generating company, the O&M expenses to the extent
          of normative O&M expenses shall be allowed. The claim of pension and Terminal
          Benefits shall be dealt as per Regulation 39.4 of these Regulations.

     41.1 The Operation and Maintenance expenses admissible to existing hydro power
          stations comprise of employee cost, Repair & Maintenance (R&M) cost and
          Administrative and General (A&G) cost. These norms for O&M exclude Pension
          and Terminal Benefits, EL encashment, and arrears to be paid to employees, taxes
          payable to the Government, and fees payable to MPERC. The generating company
          shall claim the rate, rent & taxes payable to the Government, cost of chemicals and
          consumables, fees to be paid to MPERC, EL encashment and any arrears paid to

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 143
                                                   MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

           employees separately as actual. If the O&M expenses as per norms provided in
           these Regulations are more than the actual total O&M expenses including arrears
           if any, as per audited accounts of generating company, the O&M expenses to the
           extent of normative O&M expenses shall be allowed. The claim of pension and
           Terminal Benefits shall be dealt as per Regulation 39.4 of these Regulations.”

221. Regarding water charges, Regulation 65 (2) of the Regulations, 2020 provides as follows:

         “Electricity duty, cess and water charges if payable by the Generating Company for
         generation of electricity from the power stations to the State Government, shall be
         considered and allowed by the Commission separately by considering normative
         parameters specified in these Regulations and shall be trued-up on actuals.”

222. Further, with regard to recovery of MPERC Fee and publication expenses, Regulation
     65.1 (i) of the Regulations, 2020 provides as under:

         “The application filing fee and the expenses incurred on publication of notices in the
         application for approval of tariff, may in the discretion of the Commission, be allowed
         to be recovered by the generating company directly from the beneficiaries.”

223. The Commission issued MYT Order for MPPGCL thermal and hydro power stations on
     19th May, 2021. With regard to other charges, in Para 174, 176 & 178 of the MYT Order,
     the Commission mentioned the following:

           174. The Commission observed that the O&M norms for MPPGCL’s thermal and
                hydel power stations are excluding cost of chemicals and consumables
                therefore, the petitioner is allowed to recover cost of chemicals and
                consumables in thermal and hydro power stations in accordance to Regulation
                40.1 and 41.1 respectively.

           176. The petitioner is allowed to recover the fee paid to MPERC and publication
                expenses as per Regulation 65.1 (i) of the Regulations, 2020 on submission
                of documentary evidence.

           178. “In view of the above, the petitioner is also allowed to recover the water
                charges paid to Water Resources Department, Government of MP as per
                Regulation 65.2 of the Regulations, 2020 on submission of documentary
                evidence.”

     charges have been considered in this Order:




     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                         Page 144

     Sharing of gains:

225. Regarding the performance-based truing-up of energy charges on account of controllable
     parameters, Regulation 56.1 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that the generating
     company shall work out gains based on the actual performance of applicable controllable
     parameters as under:

                    Station Heat rate
                    Secondary Fuel Oil Consumption
                    Auxiliary Energy Consumption

226. Further on sharing of gains, Regulation 56.2 provides that

     56.2     The financial gains by the generating company, on account of controllable
              parameters shall be shared between generating company and the beneficiaries
              on annual basis. The financial gains computed as per the following formulae in
              case of generating station other than hydro generating stations on account of
              operational parameters as shown in Regulation 56.1 of these Regulations shall
              be shared in the ratio of 50:50 between the generating stations and beneficiaries.
              Net Gain = (ECRN– ECRA) x Scheduled Generation

227. In view of the above Regulation, it was observed by the Commission that the generating
     company shall carry out the truing-up of tariff of generating station based on the
     controllable performance parameters like Station Heat Rate, Secondary fuel oil
     consumption and Auxiliary Energy consumption. Vide letter dated 28 th December, 2023
     the petitioner was asked to file the annual details of aforesaid performance parameters
     actually achieved vis-à-vis normative parameters under the Regulations, 2020. The
     petitioner was also asked to file the details of financial gain if any, on account of

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                           Page 145
                                                    MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

     controllable parameters and shared with the beneficiaries in light of the Regulation 56.2
     of the Regulations, 2020.

228. In response to the above query, the petitioner by affidavit dated 25th January, 2024
     submitted the following:

         As desired by Commission, the Month-wise details of Operational performance
         parameters actually achieved vis-a-vis normative parameters under the Regulations,
         2020 namely, Station Heat Rate, Secondary Oil Consumption and Auxiliary Energy
         Consumption are submitted.

         It is to submit that Regulations 56.1 & 56.2 provides that the financial gains by the
         generating company on account of controllable parameters shall be shared between
         generating company and the beneficiaries on annual basis.

         From the above, it is noted that the Regulations clearly specify that the gain achieved
         by the Company need to be shared with beneficiary. As such since MPPGCL, as a
         Company, in totality has not achieved any financial gains on account of controllable
         parameters, therefore in accordance with regulations 56.2, no sharing of gains with
         the beneficiaries is required. MPPGCL requests Commission to kindly consider the
         same.
         It is also to submit that at ATPS PH-3 minor gain of Rs. 2.25 Crores and at STPS
         PH-4 gain of Rs. 10.67 Crores approx. has been achieved individually in FY
         2022-23, whereas rests of the Power stations have not achieved any Financial Gains
         on account of Controllable parameters as per the regulation. The Power House wise
         detailed working in this regard along with Summary are submitted.

     Commission’s Analysis

229. By affidavit 15th January, 2024, the petitioner has submitted the following power station
     wise financial gain/loss on account of controllable parameters:

     Table 95: Power Station/House wise Financial Gain/Loss for FY 2022-23
                                                                    (Rs. in Crore)
                       Power Station                             Amount
      ATPS PH-3                                                                 2.25

      STPS PH-4                                                                10.67

     M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 146

      Total                                                                                     -319.79

230. On perusal of the details filed by the petitioner, it is observed that actual operating
     parameters achieved by the petitioner’s thermal power stations (except ATPS PH-3,
     STPS PH-4) during FY 2022-23 were inferior than the normative parameters, therefore,
     the petitioner incurred loss in above mentioned generating stations on account of the
     inferior performance and poor actual operating parameters achieved by it during FY
     2022-23, however, the petitioner had achieved financial gains in two of the generating
     stations namely, ATPS PH-3 and STPS PH-4.

231. Regulation 56.2 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that the financial gains by a generating
     company on account of controllable parameters shall be shared between generating
     company and the beneficiaries in the ratio of 50:50. It is, therefore directed that the gains
     achieved in above generating stations namely ATPS PH-3 and STPS PH-4 be passed
     on to the beneficiaries of the respective Generating Stations in accordance to the
     provisions under the Regulations, 2020.

232. The aforesaid Regulations do not provide for sharing of loss incurred by the generating
     company. Therefore, the loss incurred by the petitioner on the above-mentioned stations
     on account of inferior operating parameters shall not be passed on to the beneficiary.

241. This Order is for the true-up of multi-year tariff order dated 19 th May, 2021 and
     Corrigendum dated 9th September, 2021 to the extent it was applicable for FY 2022-23.
     The petitioner must take steps to implement the order after giving seven (7) days’ public
     notice in accordance with Clause 1.30 of MPERC (Details to be furnished and fee
     payable by licensee or generating company for determination of tariff and manner of
     making application) Regulations, 2004 and its amendments and recalculate its bills for
     the energy supplied to Distribution Companies of the State / M.P. Power Management
     Company Ltd. since 1st April, 2022 to 31st March, 2023. The petitioner must also provide
     information to the Commission in support of having complied with this Order. The surplus
     amount as a result of this Order shall be passed on to MP Power Management Company
     Ltd. / three Distribution Companies of the state in terms of applicable Regulation of the
     Regulations, 2020 in six equal monthly instalments during FY 2024-25 and onwards.

242. With the above directions, this petition No. 71 of 2023 is disposed of.

                                                                                   Annexure-I

    Petitioner’s Response on the comments offered by the Respondent No.1
    (MPPMCL) along with observations of the Commission


    MPPMCL’s Response
    The petitioner has earned a Non-Tariff Income to the tune of Rs. 34.07 crore,
    the same needs to be shared in terms of Regulation 58 of the Regulations,
    2020.


    Petitioner’s reply:-
    MPPGCL has indicated the total Non-Tariff Income as Rs. 68.14 Crores and has
    already shared the Non-Tariff Income as per Clause No. 58 of the Regulations,
    2020 amounting to Rs 34.07 Crores. Accordingly, the claim submitted by MPPMCL
    is already taken care in the instant petition.
    Further, a detailed explanation in regard to “Non-Tariff Income” has also been
    submitted as additional information before the Commission vide this office Letter
    dated 25.01.2024.

    MPPGCL requests the Commission to kindly consider the same.
    Observation:
    Non-Tariff Income of the petitioner for the FY 2022-23 has been considered and
    shared as per provisions of the Regulation 58 of the Regulations, 2020.


    MPPMCL’s Response
    The petitioner has claimed a sum of Rs. 23.25 crores in the true up towards
    EL Encashment under the head- of Other Charges. For the reasons that such
    expenditure of Rs. 23.25 crores is not permissible under the Regulations,
    the same should be disallowed in the true-up.


    Petitioner’s reply:-
    In the above context, MPPGCL wishes to submit to kindly refer regulation 40.1 &
    regulation 41.1 of the Regulations, 2020, which provide as under:
              “ 40.1 The Operation and Maintenance expenses admissible to the
                       thermal power stations declared under commercial operation
                       prior to 01.04.2012 comprise of employee cost, Repair &

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 157
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

                       Maintenance (R&M) cost and Administrative and General
                       (A&G) cost. These norms for O&M exclude Pension and
                       Terminal Benefits, EL encashment, and arrears to be paid to
                       employees, taxes payable to the Government, and
                       feespayable to MPERC. The generating company shall claim
                       the rate, rent & taxes payable to the Government, cost of
                       chemicals and consumables, fees to be paid to MPERC, EL
                       encashment and any arrears paid to employees separately
                       as actual. “

              “41.1 The Operation and Maintenance expenses admissible to
                    existing hydro power stations comprise of employee cost,
                       Repair & Maintenance (R&M) cost and Administrative and
                       General (A&G) cost. These norms for O&M exclude Pension
                       and Terminal Benefits, EL encashment, and arrears to be
                       paid to employees, taxes payable to the Government, and
                       fees payable to MPERC. The generating company shall claim
                       the rate, rent & taxes payable to the Government, cost of
                       chemicals and consumables, fees to be paid to MPERC, EL
                       encashment and any arrears paid to employees separately
                       as actual.”

    As evident from above regulations of the Regulations, 2020, the EL Encashment
    is allowed to be claimed separately on actual basis. Accordingly, based on the
    Audited Annual Statements of Accounts for FY 2022-23, the same is claimed on
    actual basis by MPPGCL in the instant petition.

    As such, the observation made by MPPMCL in the matter appears to be incorrect
    and is against the Regulations, 2020.

    Observation:
    The Petitioner has claimed EL encashment under Other Charges as per provisions
    of the Regulations, 2020 and the same has been considered by the Commission.
    MPPMCL’s Response

    The Petitioner has not provided details of Clean Development Mechanism
    (CDM) Benefits in terms of Regulation 59 of the said Regulations and has
    not shared the proceeds of Carbon Credit, if any, with the answering
    Respondent.

   Petitioner’s Reply:-
    In the above context, MPPGCL wishes to submit at the time of preparation of the
    DPR of SSTPP Stage-II, the CDM mechanism in India was still in the nascent

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 158
                                                 MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

    stage and further deliberation were being made. As has been observed from the
    documents available in the public domain, it appears that none of the supercritical
    thermal power plants in India received the CDM benefits possibly because the
    emission reductions due to efficient technologies viz-a-viz the base line emissions
    far out play additional emissions due to increased size of Units. Moreover, the
    CDM Methodologies Panel of United Nations Framework Convention on Climate
    Change (UNFCCC), in its 50th meeting (June 2011) had raised serious concern
    over the over-estimation of emission reductions upto a tune of 25% viz-a-viz the
    ‘Baseline Emission’. The Panel in its report even indicated that continued use of
    approved methodology ACM0013: Consolidated Baseline and Monitoring
    Methodology for New Grid Connected Fossil Fuel Fired Power Plants using a less
    GHG intensive technology, may lead to significant over-estimation of emission
    reductions and accordingly recommended to the CDM Executive Board to put the
    methodology on hold with immediate effect while initiating a revision of the
    methodology. While the ‘Hold’ never came through, there is no evidence in the
    public domain of any of Super Critical Thermal Power Plant in India receiving the
    said credits. Therefore, Clean Development Mechanism was not envisaged
    resulting in no benefit.
    MPPGCL humbly requests the Commission to kindly consider the same.

    Observation:

    In view of the Petitioner’s reply, issue of sharing the benefits does not arise.
    MPPMCL’s Response

    Contrary to the provisions of Regulation 56 of the said Regulations, the
    Petitioner has not shared the gains due to variation in norms in relation to
    Station Heat Rate, Secondary Oil Fuel and Auxiliary Consumption. The
    answering Respondent claims its respective share.

    Petitioner’s Reply:-
    In the matter, regulation 56.2 of the Regulations, 2020 provides that the financial
    gains by a generating company on account of controllable parameters shall be
    shared between generating company and the beneficiaries on annual basis.

    MPPGCL wishes to submit that the Power House wise detailed working towards
    sharing of gains, have already been submitted by MPPGCL as additional
    information before Commission vide letter No. 07-12/CS-MPPGCL/MPERC/TU
    FY 23/Pt.71 of 2023/61 dated 15.01.2024, wherein it has been submitted that
    MPPGCL, as a Company, in totality has not achieved any financial gains on
    account of controllable parameters, therefore in accordance with regulations 56.2,
    no sharing of gains with the beneficiaries is required.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                            Page 159
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

    It is requested before the Commission to kindly consider the same.

    Observation:
    As per submission of the petitioner, it is observed that it has achieved financial
    gains in two of the generating stations namely, ATPS PH-3 and STPS PH-4. It is,
    therefore directed that the gains achieved in generating stations namely ATPS
    PH-3 and STPS PH-4 be passed on to the beneficiaries of the respective
    Generating Stations in accordance to the provisions under the Regulations, 2020.

    MPPMCL’s Response
    Petitioner has separately claimed an amount of Rs. 1.16 Crores towards
    Rent, Rate & Taxes and Rs. 23.25 Crore towards EL Encashment under
    Regulations 40.1 and 41.1 of the said Regulations as Other Charges totaling
    to Rs. 208.84 Crore and the said amount totaled with the Actual O&M is less
    than the Normative O&M Expense, the same should be allowed to the extent
    of Normative O&M only. The double claim of other Charges should not be
    allowed.

    Petitioner’s reply:-
    In the above context, MPPGCL wishes to submit to kindly refer Regulation 40.1,
    Regulation 41.1 & Regulation 65.2 of the Regulations, 2020.
    As evident from aforementioned regulations of the Regulations, 2020, Rent Rates
    and Taxes, Cost of Chemicals, EL Encashment, Water Charges, MPERC Fees
    are allowed to be claimed on actual basis. Accordingly, based on the Audited
    Annual Statements of Accounts for FY 2022-23, the same is claimed on actual
    basis by MPPGCL in the instant petition.
    As such the observation made by MPPMCL in the matter appears to be incorrect
    & is against the regulations of the Regulations, 2020.
    Observation:

    Other Charges such as Rent Rates and Taxes, Cost of Chemicals and EL
    Encashment, have been considered in accordance to the Regulations, 2020 in
    this true-up order by the Commission and double counting has not taken place.
    MPPMCL’s Response

    The petitioner has claimed Additional Capitalization to the tune of Rs. 153.88
    Crore. For the reasons that the petitioner has not only given the figures in
    rupee terms in respect of additional capitalization relating to each of its
    projects but has failed to provide detailed justification of the same relating
    to the nature of capital asset, its necessity, etc., the claim in this respect
    needs to be rejected.

M.P. Electricity Regulatory Commission                                          Page 160
                                               MPPGCL True-Up Order for FY 2022-23 in P-71/2023

    Petitioner’s reply:-

    In the above context, MPPGCL wishes to submit that the Power House wise
    details of additional capitalization along with supporting documents in this regard,
    have already been submitted by MPPGCL before the Commission vide letter
    dated 25.01.2024.

    It is requested before the Commission to kindly consider the same.
    Observation:

    The additional capitalization claimed in the petition has been examined thoroughly
    by the Commission in accordance to the provisions under the Regulations, 2020,
    Annual Audited Accounts of the petitioner for FY 2022-23, Asset-cum-
    Depreciation Register for FY 2022-23 and other supplementary submissions filed
    by the petitioner and other documents placed on record by the petitioner.
    MPPMCL’s Response

    That, the petitioner has claimed the MPERC Fees, Water charges and Cost
    of chemicals for STPS Sarni Phase II and III which should be nullified as
    there is no power generation from these units. Further, these charges
    should be nullified in upcoming MYT Order FY- 25 onwards also.

    Petitioner’s reply:-
    In the above context, MPPGCL wishes to submit to kindly refer Regulation 40.1,
    Regulation 41.1 & Regulation 65.2 of the Regulations, 2020.

    As evident from regulations of the Regulations, 2020, Rent Rates and Taxes, Cost
    of Chemicals, EL Encashment, Water Charges, MPERC Fees are allowed to be
    claimed on actual basis. Accordingly, based on the Audited Annual Statements of
    Accounts for FY 2022-23, the same is claimed on actual basis by MPPGCL in the
    instant petition.
    The Units of PH-2&3 of STPS Sarni have not been decommissioned till date,
    therefore, the above charges are being claimed. MPPMCL shall appreciate that
    MPPGCL has also applied “Non-Tariff Income” amounting to Rs. 7.60 Crores for
    this power station while claiming True-up of Generation Tariff in the instant
    petition.

    Accordingly, the observation made by MPPMCL in the matter appears to be
    incorrect & is against the Provision of the Regulations, 2020.

    Observation:
    Fee Payable to MPERC for tariff determination, Water charges and Cost of Chemical
    are considered in accordance to the provision under Regulation, 2020.
